| `js/character-format.js`, `css/character-format.css` | The "Aa" (Character) and "Underline" menus of the toolbar (`CharacterFormat`): letter spacing, small capitals and the underline kind (`lineStyle` of the `underline` mark: double, dotted, dashed, wavy), four attributes of the `textStyle` mark (`letterSpacing` "2.5pt", `fontVariant` "small-caps", `textShadow` "1.5pt 1.5pt 2pt #00000066", `textStroke` "0.75pt #ff0000") written as inline style (`letter-spacing`, `font-variant`, `text-shadow`, `-webkit-text-stroke`); `parseShadow` and `parseStroke` (read by the exports and the import); bounded (-50 to 500 pt, px and em converted to points); `createExtension`, `declarations` (the single writer for the import), `spacingPt` and `smallCapsOf` (what the exports read), `smallCapsPieces` (the PDF draws small capitals: capitals at 70%), `readState`, `apply` (one transaction), `wireMenus` (the wiring of the two menus: each row applies at once, the state of the targeted text is read when a menu opens; Superscript and Subscript are the two icons of the "Aa" menu). |
| `js/heading-numbering.js` | Heading numbering, the same for the editor, Reading mode, the PDF and the Word file. |
| `js/toc-links.js` | The lines of a table of contents are links: a click brings the heading they name to the top of the visible area (the editor preview, which also puts the cursor there, and Reading mode, by keyboard too). |
| `js/anchors.js` | Bookmarks: a name placed on a top-level paragraph or heading (`data-anchor` and `data-anchor-name` attributes, editor flag), the address derived from the name, the list of targets for an "In this document" link (in a presentation, every slide first, under its rank and title: `targets().slides`), the jump to the target; what the exports read (`indexElements`). |
| `js/bookmark-dialog.js` | The "Bookmark" window: place, rename or remove the bookmark of the block where the cursor is. |
| `js/email-plain-text.js` | The e-mail template only writes what the `mailto:` link carries: the bold, italic, underline, alignment and heading-level keys do nothing, typed Markdown signs (« # » included) stay text, pasting drops the formatting (a heading becomes a plain line, pasted plain text keeps its blank lines), formatting left over from an older template is no longer shown. |

### Variables and conditions (21 files, 8,800 lines)

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `js/variables.js` | The `#` list, the value of a bubble (another table, a path of references, a calculation), the window for matching keys between tables. |
| `js/variable-format.js` | A bubble's format: number (currency, percentage, decimals), date (the day and time, on a 24- or 12-hour clock, in the zone of a "Date and time" column; `timeText` also writes the time of the "Current time" chip), Yes / No, list, number in words; and "Like in Grist": a Grist column's display format translated into a bubble setting. |
| `js/variable-modal.js` | What a bubble's windows have in common. |
| `js/variable-condition.js`, `js/variable-loop.js`, `js/variable-list.js`, `js/variable-linked-attrs.js`, `js/variable-calc.js`, `js/variable-column.js` | A bubble's windows: Condition, Loop (including the zone around the bubble, for a loop in a loop, and the choice of rows: attached to the displayed row, or all the rows of the table, the widget's, those of the neighbouring table it is linked to, or the whole table; under “The variable, in its cell”, the note saying that the other variables of the table row do not follow; `rowStatus` and `openRow`, what the table bar's “Repeat the row” button knows about the targeted row; under "If no row", the text shown is the field with variables of "Otherwise show": `mountEmptyField`), List, Other attributes (which also opens on an `@` chip, on the row of its record), Calculation, Column. |
| `js/variable-otherwise.js` | A bubble's "otherwise" (the "Otherwise show" row of the Condition window, the same field with variables as for a value or a block): its saved form - the old object (one variable set only by its "Before" / "After", five attributes) or, for any other content, a field string in the `data-otherwise` attribute -, what it writes when the condition is not met, and its resolution before the bubbles in Reading mode and in the exports. |
| `js/field-editor.js`, `js/field-codec.js` | The text fields of E-mail mode (Subject, To, Cc, Bcc) and of the PDF file name: a one-line editor that places the same bubbles as the document, with their toolbar and windows, and the user's date, time, email and name chips; the stored value stays plain text as long as no bubble has a setting and no chip is placed. Each field keeps the history of its keystrokes while it has the focus; its session - from focus to leaving - leaves one marker in the template's Undo and Redo (`snapshot`, `restore`, `settle`). The same field is used in the condition window: the "Otherwise show" of a conditional value, a conditional block or a variable (`FieldEditor.attach` with `hostWindow`, `windowEl`, `multiline`, `keepSpaces`, `limit`; a draft of the window, outside the template's Undo markers; `FieldCodec.linesOf` and `toStoredLines` for its lines). |
| `js/formula.js` | The calculation engine of a "Calculation" bubble (a pure module: no DOM, no Grist). |
| `js/condition-rules.js`, `js/condition-fields.js` | Evaluation of "column, operator, value or other column" rules (a pure module) and their fields shared by all the windows. |
| `js/loop-rules.js`, `js/list-split.js` | The loop engine over linked rows, or over all the rows of a table (`all`: the widget's rows, those of the neighbouring table it is linked to by "Select by" - its saved filters and sort, `ViewSource.linkedRows` - or the whole table; `allSourceFor`) (it also gives each zone copy the rank of its turn, which the "Row number" chip writes, and it expands the outer zones first: a zone inside a zone reads the rows of the current row of the zone around it; the "If no row" text (`emptyText`) is a field with variables, like "Otherwise show": `emptyNodes`); "One document per value". |
| `js/conditional-text.js`, `js/conditional-checkbox.js`, `js/conditional-value.js` | Conditional block, checkbox and value. The value and the block have an "otherwise": a field with variables, the same as the email's Subject, written in their place when the condition is not met - text and variables typed with "#", plain text as long as no variable has a setting and field HTML otherwise (the node's `otherwise` attribute, the "Otherwise show" row of the condition window, the `TEXT_OTHERWISE` table of `js/variable-condition.js`; read by `ConditionalText.otherwiseText`, `otherwiseItems`, `otherwiseValueItems` and `otherwiseLines`, written by `ConditionalValue.resolve` and `ConditionalText.resolve`: one line for the value, one paragraph per line for the block). A variable of the otherwise can be clicked: its bar is that of a page variable, and its "Condition" opens a second window over the first (`createWindow(level)` of `js/variable-condition.js`, `Layers.above` of `js/layers.js`, called by `js/modal-base.js` whenever a window opens). A block that ends its table cell, column or list item has nothing after it to hold the cursor: a click in the container's free area, below the frame or to its right, adds an empty paragraph there (`ConditionalText.addParagraphAfter`, the `conditionalTextAfter` plugin of `createConditionalTextNode` in `js/editor-nodes.js`); a block that opens its container has nothing before it: a click above the frame adds an empty paragraph there (`ConditionalText.addParagraphBefore`, same plugin). |
| `js/space-gap.js` | The spaces around what writes nothing: a removed conditional value, or a bubble that is empty, hidden, zero-hidden or has no row in its loop, no longer double the spaces typed around it and leave none at a paragraph's edge or before a comma (Reading view, all the exports, headers and footers, e-mail fields). |

### Macro-templates (2 files, 680 lines)

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `js/macro-templates.js` | Choosing and assembling a macro-template's appendices (no DOM); with "Follow each template", writing the page of each assembled template: its orientation and, for a presentation, its slide format. |
| `js/macro-editor.js` | The screen for creating a macro-template, and its summary (documents, grids and presentations are assembled; "Grid", "Slides" and "Landscape" tags). |

### Page and layout (20 files, 6,100 lines)

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `js/page-layout.js` | Margins, orientation, size, watermark and kept colors of each template: the single source of the content width. `boundedMarginsMm` returns what `setMarginsMm` would keep for given margins, without setting anything (the presets of Settings > Margins use it to know which one is in place). `getSlideRatios`, `slideRatioOf` and `isSlideFormat`: the four ratios of a slide (16:9, 4:3, 16:10, 1:1, custom sizes with a 19.05 cm short side), which a presentation's Page menu offers and which the zoom and Reading recognise. |
| `js/action-history.js` | The actions that reach beyond the open template, in Undo and Redo (`ActionHistory`): the document's default-template ★, deleting a template, copying a template ("Save as…", a gallery template: undoing removes it after a question), the person's arrangement (pins, folders, folders collapsed by default: `js/template-arrangement.js`), the link rules between tables (`js/link-rule-history.js`), the person's settings - the theme, the bubbles and chips, the insert character, the mail program, the keyboard keys, the abbreviation trigger character, the view's default template, the column of the last export date, the template according to the row, the rights setting (`js/settings-history.js`) -, the list of abbreviations (`js/expansion-history.js`); the language, which reloads the page, stays outside Undo. The editor's history is the open template's and `Editor.setHTML` empties it at every loaded template: the module keeps what is needed to undo and redo each action (`add({ undo, redo })`, two async functions that return the status-corner text and receive the action's number, set once the action is done), `HistoryMarkers.recordAction` files its marker in the history, `reseed` files it again in the new history at every loaded template (not that of an action being undone), `sync` undoes or redoes the action when the history gives its marker back (actions run one after the other; an Undo followed at once by a Redo cancel each other without writing). `conflict(message)`: an action that can no longer be undone because someone else changed the state since writes nothing and says so. `forget(id)` drops an action nothing depends on any more (the PDF whose thread it aimed at is no longer on screen). Thirty actions at most. |
| `js/template-arrangement.js` | The person's template arrangement in Undo and Redo (`TemplateArrangement`): a template's pin, the folder it is filed in, the folder's "collapsed by default" state. The template list and the "Organize my templates" window write through `pin`, `moveTo` and `foldByDefault` instead of writing to `TemplatePreferences`: the gesture is written as before, then set on the `js/action-history.js` stack. Undo gives the state back as it was before, redo as it was after; a template deleted meanwhile is not filed again (nothing is written, the status corner says so); a gesture that changes nothing leaves no step. |
| `js/link-rule-history.js` | The link rules between tables in Undo and Redo (`LinkRuleHistory`): the key window (when inserting a variable from another table or through the pencil of "Linked tables"), the bin of "Linked tables" and the link set up on its own for a Reference column write through `track(targetTable, work)` instead of writing alone: the gesture is written as before (`GristAPI.saveLinkRule`, `deleteLinkRule`), then what it changed for the linked table is set on the `js/action-history.js` stack, with the link before and the link after. Undo gives the link back as it was before (none: it is removed), redo as it was after, by the same write as the gesture, from any template. A link changed since (set again, followed in a rename) is not overwritten and a link is not given back to a table that is gone: nothing is written, the status corner says so. A gesture that is cancelled, unchanged or refused leaves no step. |
| `js/settings-history.js` | The person's settings in Undo and Redo (`SettingsHistory`): the theme, the “Default” or “Formatting” choice for bubbles and chips, the trigger key (the insert character) and the mail program of E-mail mode, in `js/settings.js`, the keyboard keys of `js/shortcuts-panel.js` (one key, or all at once: `KEYS_SETTING`) and the abbreviation trigger character of `js/text-expansion-settings.js`, write through `track(setting, work)` instead of writing alone: the gesture is written as before (`js/person-store.js` and the attribute of `<html>`), then the value before and the value after are set on the `js/action-history.js` stack. The settings of the View and Access tabs - the view's template (`js/view-template.js`), the column of the last export date (`js/export-date.js`), the template according to the row (`js/row-template.js`) and the per-person rights (`js/access-rights.js`: the table, its columns, the Clean reading box) - go through it too: they are widget options, a draft in Grist until its "Save", and undoing them writes them the same way. A setting is `{ read, write, say, locked? }`: what is in force, the gesture's write, the status-corner text (`say(value, before)`: `before` lets it name, for the keys, the action that changed), and `locked()` when a read-only account can no longer write it (undo then does nothing and says so). `record(setting, before, after)` sets the step of a gesture whose caller holds both values (a rule's run of typing). Undo gives the value before back, redo the value after, by the same write as the gesture, from any template. A setting changed since (another tab, GFS) is not overwritten: nothing is written, the status corner says so. A choice that changes nothing, or that the storage refuses, leaves no step. |
| `js/expansion-history.js` | The person's abbreviations in Undo and Redo (`ExpansionHistory`): the three gestures of the Settings > Shortcuts > Abbreviations list (`js/text-expansion-settings.js`: "Add", "Save", "Delete") go through `add`, `update` and `remove` instead of calling `TextExpansion` alone: the gesture is written as before (a row of the `Publipostage_Abreviations` table), then the abbreviation before and the abbreviation after are set on the `js/action-history.js` stack. Undo gives the abbreviation before back (none: the row is removed), redo the one after, by the same write as the gesture, from any template. A removed abbreviation comes back under its old row number if it is free in the whole table (the table is shared by all the people of the document), otherwise under a new number that the following steps follow (`line`). An abbreviation changed or removed since, or whose name another abbreviation has taken, is not overwritten: nothing is written, the status corner says so. A gesture that is refused, or changes nothing, leaves no step. |
| `js/history-markers.js` | What is not text in Undo and Redo (`HistoryMarkers`): the template's page - size, orientation, margins, watermark, kept colors - lives outside the document, so outside the editor's history; each page gesture leaves a marker there, a step that does not change the document and keeps the page before and after, filed between the keystrokes, and a transaction observer gives the page back to Ctrl + Z, Ctrl + Y and the toolbar buttons. Eight domains: "page", "headerFooter" (a writing session in the header or footer), "macro" (a macro template's composition), "fields" (the fields outside the text: the email's Subject, To, Cc, Bcc and the PDF name), "comments" (the messages of a comment thread, rows of the Grist table), "exportChoices" (the open template's export type, quality and rows), "name" (the name the pencil gives the open template: `js/main.js` declares it with `define`, it alone holds the field) and "actions" (the library's actions, `recordAction`: the marker carries only the action's number, `reset` files the markers of the done actions in the new history; `recordUndoneAction` files the marker of an UNDONE action where Redo finds it, for one whose undoing reloaded the template: the removed copy); `begin(domain, before, scope)` aims at a part of the state (one thread), `commit({ join: true })` files the marker in the event on top of the history; `keep` and `resume` hold the history across the reload of the same macro template (and give back the history from before the mark of a thread closed without a message, or that of the blank document when undoing a deletion reopens the template: `redoDepth` says what it keeps to redo), `flush` files the session of a field that is still open before Undo or Redo undoes the history. The module also adds a ProseMirror plugin, `historyKeepsLinks$`: its `filterTransaction` sets `preventAutolink` on the transaction of a Ctrl + Z or a Ctrl + Y, so that Tiptap's Link extension (which reads every transaction) does not remake a link on an address followed by a space that the history has just given back; what is typed keeps its links. |
| `js/page-sections.js` | Reads the page markers of a macro template (the orientation, portrait or landscape, and a presentation's format) so that each template keeps its own page in the PDF, Word, reading mode and print output; the HTML export, which has no pages, leaves the start marker out. |
| `js/page-section-view.js` | Shows the turned pages of a document in the editor: a sheet as wide as the widest page, each page at its own size, the text of each page in its own width. |
| `js/page-sense-edit.js` | Writes the page breaks that turn one page or the whole document, from the Page button's menu: the plan of breaks to add, change or remove, from the pagination's cuts. |
| `js/page-layer.js` | "On every page" images and the watermark. |
| `js/orientation-toggle.js`, `js/page-size-dialog.js`, `js/watermark-dialog.js` | The Page menu and its windows: orientation ("Whole document" or "This page" for a document, and a macro template's "Follow each template") and size, "Custom size…", "Watermark…". In a presentation, the title says "Slide" and the menu offers the 16:9, 4:3, 16:10 and 1:1 ratios (`syncRatios`). |
| `js/page-zoom.js` | On-screen page zoom, in Edit and Reading modes (corner pill, Fit, Ctrl + wheel); display only, the exports keep the real dimensions. With GFS's bar (directive 025): the open pill, and the ▶ to its left (`data-pp-zoom-companion`), step aside by what the bar bites (`stepAside`, `--pp-gfs-shift-x` and `-y`); otherwise nothing moves. In `js/main.js`, the fit reads first and writes afterwards (`planPageFitZoom`, `commitPageFitZoom`): the factor is only written once both containers and the table-row probe have been read, so the style of the whole document is not redone at each adjustment. |
| `js/header-footer-preview.js` | Header and footer editing, paginated preview (cuts between two blocks placed all at once then read back one by one, `placeSimpleSeamsAtOnce`: every run of plain cuts, including those between two cuts of a table or a zone; those of a table or a zone, and any cut that is not where it was predicted, placed page by page, `placePaginationSeam`); each reserve checked against the block that opens the next page and corrected (`placeReserve`; its rule names the page-break marker when that is what ends the page, `seamSubject`: `.page-break-marker:nth-child(N)` and not `*:nth-child(N)`, which makes the browser restyle every block each time a sheet is added), then watched: a block that slides afterwards starts the calculation again (`reservesHold`, `recomputeIfMoved`). A block that is not rendered (`display: none`: the heading-numbering setting) does not count in `computePageBreaks` and carries no cut: its rectangle is null at the origin of the screen, not of the text. A change that only touches free frames (zero-height anchors) does not redo the pagination: `paginationHolds` compares the text and the page's top-level blocks with those the last pagination read (`paginatedDoc`, `paginatedBlocks`); a frame added, removed or carrying a layered image (`carriesPageImage`) redoes it. `breaksOf(tiptapEl)` returns the page cuts of a text laid outside the editor, at the current page (the cut of a document page into slides, `extensions/diapositives/page-slides.js`) The pagination timer (`schedulePaginationRecompute`) computes nothing for a hidden editor (Reading): the return to Edit paginates again (`Editor.refreshLayout`). When a template opens, the check of the `top` of the layered images of pages 2 and on against the page grid (`reconcileLayerImagesWithGrid`, in a scale-1 window) keeps its "nothing to fix" verdict per person (`PersonStore`, key `pp_layer_checks`: a short list of signatures of the loaded text, the page, the header and footer, the versions of js/ and css/, the screen density and the browser; `layerCheckSignature`, `layerCheckHolds`, `layerCheckKeep`) and does not reopen its window for a template already checked; with no verdict, or a refused one, it measures as before. |
| `js/table-page-cut.js` | Where a table is cut between two pages. |
| `js/zone-page-cut.js` | Where a two-column zone is cut between two pages, column by column like the PDF (the editor and Reading mode). |
| `js/sheet-layout.js`, `js/sheet-assembly-dialog.js` | "Assemble before printing": the geometry of a sheet and its window. |

### Reading (2 files, 1,900 lines)

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `js/reader-mode.js` | The document resolved for a row and paginated (the same cut calculation as the editor: `placeSimpleSeamsAtOnce`, then `placeSeam`, the same reserves checked and watched, `watchGeometry`; `layOutSheets` suspends scroll anchoring for the time of the layout); `preview()` also serves all the exports. Entering Reading draws the page only once: `refitToContent` reads the fit factor again once the page is laid out (a scrollbar that takes width changes it) and only redoes the layout; `renderRecord` starts reading the column types while the HTML is being built, and `resolveReaderBody` starts the variable images, the QR codes, the charts and the chips together. `layoutSeams` places each run of plain page breaks at once (`placeSimpleSeamsAtOnce(pass, offsets, start)`, which resumes at cut `start`) and the cuts inside a table or a zone one by one (`placeSeam`), then resumes with the next plain run; a batch that places nothing stops the batches of that calculation (`pass.atOnce`). |
| `js/reader-guide.js` | The guide shown when no row is chosen, and the "Give this widget full access" card the editor shows in place of the document when Grist does not give it full access. |

### Grid mode (6 files, 4,400 lines)

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `js/grid-editor.js` | The "grid" template: a single spreadsheet-like table, with adjustable columns and rows. |
| `js/grid-table.js` | Reading a table pasted from Excel, Google Sheets or LibreOffice. |
| `js/grid-xlsx-import.js` | Importing an `.xlsx` workbook into a grid. |
| `js/table-borders.js` | The border rule, written once for the editor, Reading mode, the PDF and the Excel file. |
| `js/xlsx-number-format.js` | The text Excel would show for a number or date format. |
| `js/linked-table.js` | A document's table linked to a Grid template: the link attribute and the template's token that makes it count, picking the template in the list, the marker in the page, the content rules for the cells and the locks (track changes, read-only), updating the table from the template, sending it to the template and opening the template (with the way back to the document, which `js/main.js` carries), then the document following its template: the table's base (what the document and the template said at the last sync), the plan drawn from it (update, send, or a disagreement), the sync on opening and at every read of the automatic save (`followLinkedModels` in `js/main.js`), the sending of the changed tables to their templates with the document's save - in the same write as its row (`Templates.save`), the editor's base moving only once Grist agrees - and the reading of the template by the outputs that read saved documents. |

### Exports (26 files, 17,800 lines)

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `js/export-common.js` | Helpers shared by the exports, and the script loader (cdnjs and jsDelivr with an integrity hash, or the widget's own site; anything else is refused), which also loads, as a module with its integrity hash, the file GFS assembles for live writing. |
| `js/export-engines.js` | Loads, on the first export rather than at startup, the widget's export engines (PDF, PDF merging, sheet assembly, Word, PowerPoint, Excel, Excel number formats, Markdown, HTML), declared in `index.html` by inert tags. |
| `js/image-io.js` | Reading images, for insertion (paste, address) as well as for the PDF, Word and Excel exports: a file, a Blob or an address becomes a Blob, a `data:` URI or a PNG (`fetch`, `new Image()`); and, for the "Light" PDF quality, an image brought down to 150 points per inch in its box (`lighten`, a canvas). |
| `js/pdf-export.js` | The vector PDF (pdfmake): the biggest file of the widget. A macro template that mixes page formats (A4 pages and a slide) is laid out there run by run of pages of the same format (pdfmake only does one format per file), the numbering, header and footer staying those of the whole document; `PdfMerge.joinParts` joins the runs. |
| `js/print-export.js` | Browser print (the "Browser print" quality of the "Export type" menu): the Reading view rendered (`ReaderMode.renderInto`), cut into sheets of the template's exact page size (`css/print.css`), then handed to `window.print()`. One way to print, on its own or hosted by Grist Factory Suite (whose isolated frame does not let a hidden frame be read): the widget's own page - a shadow-tree container put in the page, which `@media print` shows alone while printing and which is removed at `afterprint`. No library to load; one row at a time, 60 pages at most. `stage`: the same rendering, without sheets or printing, in a single copy moved from one page to the next (the slide show of `extensions/diapositives/slide-show.js`). |
| `js/pdf-fonts.js`, `js/pdf-fonts-extra.js`, `js/pdf-fonts-boxes.js`, `js/pdf-fonts-symbols.js` | The PDF fonts, data loaded on the first PDF (`pdf-fonts.js`, `pdf-fonts-boxes.js` and `pdf-fonts-symbols.js` are generated by scripts of the development repository, which are not published here). |
| `js/pdf-glyph-fallback.js` | Character-by-character font fallback. |
| `js/pdf-merge.js` | A single PDF for all rows (pdf-lib), and for the runs of pages of different formats of one document (`joinParts`). |
| `js/pdf-delivery.js` | Where a PDF the app has just made goes (`PdfDelivery`): “Download” (the origin), “Save to GFS” or both, chosen by each person (“Export” menu > “PDF destination”, key `pp_export_destination`, undone by Ctrl + Z). The choice only counts when GFS hosts the app and answers; otherwise the PDF is downloaded as before and the two rows that save are greyed with their reason. `deliver` hands each PDF to GFS through `GfsLink.deposit` (GFS instruction 043: one file at a time, one lot, `suivants` counting down to 0, never a folder), counts GFS's four answers (added, replaced, copy “(1)”, refused by the person) and downloads as before whatever GFS did not keep; `describe` turns that into the status-corner sentences. |
| `js/docx-export.js` | The Word file (docx). |
| `js/pptx-export.js` | The PowerPoint file (`.pptx`, beta, for a presentation): a row's resolved HTML, with each slide's marker (`SlidesEntry.outputHtml`, `keepProps` option), written by hand as OOXML into a JSZip archive - a minimal template (theme, master, layout), then, per slide (`buildSlide`), objects that PowerPoint can edit: title and text boxes (lists, links, formatting), tables (`tableXml`), images, preset shapes and straight connectors (`frameXml`, `connectorXml`, from `js/free-frame.js`; a frame behind the text is written before the slide's text, `shapes.behindFrames`), background, transition (`transitionXml`) and speaker notes (`notesSlideXml`). Positions are measured on the real rendering (`ExportCommon.attachMeasureHost`). No extra library; `getPptxBlobForRecord` also serves the one-file-per-row ZIP archive. A hidden slide is written `show="0"`, the automatic advance `advTm` (with or without an effect). Object animations (`FrameAnim`) are written as `p:timing`: one step per click, one group per start, the appear, fade, fly, wipe and zoom presets (`timingXml`). |
| `js/xlsx-export.js` | The Excel file of a grid (ExcelJS). A created chart becomes a native Excel chart there: `addNativeChart` notes the chart (its part comes from `DocxExport.chartPartsOf`), then `workbookToBlob` writes the "Chart data" sheet (`writeChartData`) and adds the chart's parts and its anchor in its sheet's drawing to ExcelJS's archive (`injectCharts`); the image stays when that fails. |
| `js/markdown-export.js` | Markdown (`.md`, CommonMark and GFM): a row's resolved HTML read node by node, what Markdown can write written as Markdown and the rest as inline HTML, with no library. Its second use, `exportTemplate` / `getMarkdownBlobForTemplate` ("Markdown (template)"), writes the template itself: a variable with no settings as `{{Table.Column}}`, the other merge elements as inline HTML with their `data-*`. |
| `js/pptx-import.js` | PowerPoint import, the reverse of `js/pptx-export.js`: a `.pptx` read by hand (OPC package, JSZip, `DOMParser`, loaded on the first import) becomes the TEXT of a new presentation (the HTML the editor itself writes: `FreeFrame.html`, `SlideProps.html`), the page it takes (16:9, 4:3, portrait or a free size) and a REPORT of what it did not carry over as is; it touches neither the editor nor the page, `onImportPptx` in `js/main.js` opens the template, unnamed and not yet saved. Each object becomes a free frame, a shape or a line at its place to the point (PowerPoint centres a line on the edge, the editor draws it inside: the box is moved out by half the thickness; every preset shape keeps its kind and its settings, `a:avLst` read by `adjustsOf`, and its frame holds everything it draws, `frameOfShape` through `ShapeGeometry.presetFrame`, placed so that a rotated shape finds its place again; a freeform becomes a counted rectangle; a solid fill or line keeps its transparency, `inkOf`, the average color of a gradient or a pattern being blended into the page's background; its dash, its cap, its compound type and the arrowheads of its two ends - their shape, width and length - are carried, the ones the theme gives through `lnRef` included, `lineOf`), with the layout's then the master's inheritance for position, size, colour, alignment, bullets, line spacing and the spaces before and after (`spacingOf`: a percentage of the single line, exact points related to the paragraph's tallest size; the space before a box's first paragraph and the one after its last are left out); the master's decor is copied behind the text; tables are the editor's own, at their height through empty lines. Nothing makes the import fail: an unreadable object is counted in the report, only a file that is not a presentation, a pre-2007 `.ppt`, a presentation without slides or a file over 150 MB is refused. The report says everything (`REPORT_KEYS`, one line per kind, 50 kinds, `pptx.import.report.<kind>` in `js/i18n.js`): what does not arrive, then what the file asks for and the editor cannot yet do (tabs, character spacing, shadow, fonts...), counted once per object, never for what the editor does itself; a job that teaches the import to carry one of these removes its line. `customKindOf` reads an `a:custGeom` (guides, settings, paths, text rectangle) and freezes it into a freeform; too big for a name, it stays a counted rectangle. `routedConnector` reads a stepped or curved connector's route (the standard's paths for its box and handles) and its arrowheads (`ShapeGeometry.endPolygon`) as a freeform. `finishedPicture` (with `imageAdjustments`, `pictureOutline`, `pictureEffects`) does a picture's adjustments, shape, border, shadow and glow in its pixels, and grows its box by what overflows. `show="0"` and `advTm` are carried into the slide's marker (`transitionOf`); `customArtFits` refuses a freeform whose drawing would no longer survive the HTML cleaner. Object animations: an entrance or exit of appear, fade, fly, wipe or zoom on a whole object becomes its animation (`timingOf`, `animationOf`, `animateFrames`); the sequence's order, when it differs from the text, is written as `n` (`settleAnimations`); the rest is counted. The file's theme (`themeOf`: name, background and text through the master's color map, the first level of the title style's color, six accents, two fonts matched to the editor's, Roboto by default) is returned with the page (`page.theme`) and becomes the presentation's theme (js/slide-theme.js); a slide that shows the theme's background does not write its own (`slideHtml`). |
| `js/markdown-import.js` | Markdown import, the reverse of the previous one: an `.md` read by marked (loaded from cdnjs on the first import, with its integrity hash), put back into the form the editor itself writes (task lists, callouts, footnotes, table of contents, page break, images, alignments), then cleaned by `HtmlSanitize`; the header and footer written between comments become the document's; `{{Table.Column}}` come back as variable bubbles and the merge elements that "Markdown (template)" writes as HTML get their settings back. |
| `js/html-export.js` | HTML (`.html`, for a website): a row's resolved HTML, which already is HTML, cleaned of what only serves the widget (allow-lists of tags, attributes and style declarations, nothing that can run) and accompanied by a stylesheet cut down to what the document uses. Two outputs of the same content: the complete file to upload (`getHtmlBlobForRecord`) and the content alone to paste (the `fragment` option, whose stylesheet only touches `.pp-doc`). The text flows (no pages, no margins, no page numbers, no watermark), the header and the footer are written once; images go in as data URIs, except those from another web address. No library. |
| `js/mailto-export.js` | The `mailto:` link of E-mail mode, and the mail program that opens it (Standard or Zimbra, a personal setting in Settings > Email). A cell value adds no field and no line break to it: everything is encoded, the subject stays on one line and an address's line break separates two recipients. |
| `js/batch-failures.js` | The windows of batch exports and PDFs: the one that opens at the end of a batch and lists the rows that could not be generated, with their reason (the error raised, a kind of template the export cannot make, or a protected PDF's missing or unusable row password); the one that warns that a PDF has a blank page before it is saved ("Cancel" or "Export anyway"), and its end-of-batch list (on top of the shared input and confirmation window). |
| `js/batch-scope.js` | The rows of a batch export: the ones of the neighbouring table the widget is linked to by "Select by" (according to its saved filters and sort, read by `js/view-source.js`), the ones the widget shows in Grist (its filters, its sort), or the whole table; when the view only shows part of the table, or the widget cannot read the linked view, the question is asked and names the view; `linkedViewRows` gives the rows of the neighbouring view, minus those the widget hides (the loop reads them too); `onViewChange` tells when Grist says the widget's rows changed (the reading of a loop over all the rows is redrawn). |
| `js/view-source.js` | The rows a Grist view shows, read in the document: a widget linked by "Select by" to a view of the same table (whose rows it does not receive) gets from `linkedRows` the rows of that view, in its order, according to its SAVED filters and sort (an unsaved filter is only a draft in the browser); or is told why it cannot (relative dates, a table filtered by another widget, a widget it cannot identify). It is also the engine of filters (values, number and date ranges) and sort that the chart shares. |
| `js/export-choices.js` | The person's export choices, kept per template: the type (PDF, Protected PDF, Word, PowerPoint, Excel, Markdown, Markdown (template), HTML (file to upload), HTML (content to paste)), the PDF quality and "a single row / all rows". The toolbar's three hidden lists remain what `js/main.js` reads to export; the preferences table (`js/template-preferences.js`) keeps them from one computer to the next, a read-only account keeps them for the session only. |
| `js/pdf-protection.js` | The "Protected PDF" (a type of the "Export type" menu) without any interface: the cleaned settings (the three ways to give the password - typed, a shared one saved in the document, or one per row -, three permissions, file properties), the password of a row read from its cell, the random "owner" password drawn when only permissions are set, and the document fields pdfmake reads (AES-256 encryption). |
| `js/pdf-protection-dialog.js` | The "Protected PDF" window (on top of the shared window), opened before the export of one row and of a ZIP archive: it returns the settings or nothing. It offers the three ways to give the password, says where the shared password is written in the document and the risk of a plain-text password, and reads then writes that shared password through `js/templates.js`. No password is kept in the browser; only the chosen way, the permissions, the author and the password column are. |

### Interface foundations and settings (18 files, 7,900 lines)

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `js/dom.js` | The common interface building blocks and the only place that parses markup (`Dom.setMarkup`, `Dom.inertTemplate`), loaded first. |
| `js/layers.js`, `js/viewport-fit.js` | The stacking order of menus and windows; the interface kept inside a small panel. Tooltips (`fitTooltip`) also keep out of GFS's bar zone (shifted sideways, or dropped when the bar is at the top), and every popup, panel or list placed by `left` and `top` fits to it through `avoidBar(el, anchor)` (`GfsBar.settle`: it slides, drops or moves up, or is capped and scrolls; `releaseBar` gives the cap back before measuring); `placePopup` settles its still-shown popups by itself when the bar changes. |
| `js/lock-reasons.js` | Says WHY a button or a menu row is greyed: the sentence is the hover tooltip and, on click, what the status corner writes (`LockReasons.set(element, cause, sentence)`; one cause at a time, the most general wins: rights, PDF reading, mode, grid, linked table, explanation, state; `wire` stops the click for the first five, and the earlier `title` is given back to the last one to be un-greyed). The sentences are the `lock.*` keys of `js/i18n.js`. |
| `js/modal-base.js`, `js/dialogs.js` | The common base of windows; inputs and confirmations (in place of `prompt` and `confirm`). In Grist Factory Suite, the windows' backdrop (`css/modal-base.css`) takes the room GFS's bar occupies at its edge as padding: the window's box centres in what is left. |
| `js/search-select.js` | The drop-down list with search, for every choice of column, table or template, with the walk down into a Reference's columns. |
| `js/icons.js` | The SVG icons. |
| `js/i18n.js` | The French and English translations, and the starting language: GFS's when it hosts the widget (`gfsLang`), otherwise the one the person chose, otherwise the browser's. |
| `js/person-store.js` | What the widget keeps for each person (theme, bubbles and chips of the editor, trigger keys and shortcuts, mail program of E-mail mode, autosave, assembly before printing, zoom, protected PDF, destination of exported PDFs, checks of layered images): the only file that reads and writes these preferences (`PersonStore.get`, `set`, `remove`, `ready`, `onLoaded`). Hosted by GFS, which gives it the app's space, the widget reads GFS's copy and sends it every change (GFS's isolated frame refuses the browser's storage); alone, or with a GFS that has no space, it is the browser's storage. Nothing is written before GFS's context arrives, and reading never writes. Loaded right after `js/gfs-link.js`, before any other script. |
| `js/settings.js` | The Settings panel: language, theme (and, under it, the “Default” or “Formatting” choice for bubbles and chips: `setBubbleMarks`), trigger key, mail program of E-mail mode, margins (four presets and the chain that links the four margins: `MARGIN_PRESETS`, `applyMargins`, `marginsLinked`), credits. The theme, the bubbles and chips, the trigger key and the mail program are undone by Ctrl + Z (`js/settings-history.js`). Changing the language or the key reloads the page, which reopens the template you were on: its identifier goes through the address (`#pp-open=<id>`, `reloadKeepingTemplate`), read then erased at startup (`takeReopenId`). Hosted by GFS, the language is GFS's: both language boxes are greyed with the reason (`applyLanguageLock`), and a change made in GFS reloads the page the same way (`followGfsLanguage`; GFS's language goes through the address, `&pp-lang=fr`). |
| `js/save-reminder.js` | The "Save" reminder of the Settings View and Access tabs: the line to the left of "Close" after a change, switched off by Grist's "Revert" (which says nothing of a click on "Save"), and the tabs then take back the saved setting. |
| `js/shortcuts.js`, `js/shortcuts-panel.js` | The customizable keyboard shortcuts and their list. Changing, removing or resetting a key (or all of them at once) is undone by Ctrl + Z: `Shortcuts.chosen` and `restore`, `KEYS_SETTING` of `js/shortcuts-panel.js`. Under an open window, Ctrl + Z, Ctrl + Y and Ctrl + Shift + Z are taken without running anything, so that Chrome's native Ctrl + Z does not undo the last typing behind the window (except in a text field of the window): `holdBehindWindow`. |
| `js/first-contact.js` | The first-contact window, when the widget doesn't start as expected. |
| `js/whats-new.js` | The what's-new window of a version (images in `img/news/`): a carousel of ten screens and its five-screen “Exporting, in detail” window, opened by the “What’s new” line of Settings > Credits, and by itself once per browser the first time the widget is opened after a version that has news (`WhatsNew.openOnFirstConnection`, called by `js/main.js`; the last version closed is the `pp_news_seen` key of `PersonStore`). |
| `js/clean-reading.js` | Clean reading (without the top bar) and the two tickable rows of the Reading mode button's menu, "Standard reading" (at the start) or "Clean reading": the button, and Alt+L, do what the ticked row says, like the Export button; opened straight away for read-only people, Grist Viewer accounts included, when the Settings box is ticked. |
| `js/version.js` | The version number. |

### Grist Factory Suite extensions (24 files, 9,800 lines)

The numbers in the title count only the files of `js/`, the base of these features; the 47 files of `extensions/`, which are
the extensions themselves, are described below and counted in "The size".

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `js/gfs-link.js` | The link with Grist Factory Suite when it hosts the widget (the address carries `gfs=`): the context asked at startup (the active extensions, the source of their libraries, the widget's address, the opening asked for, the language, the app's space for preferences, GFS's bar and the chosen ambiance), the `extensions` event, the `langue` event (`language()`, `onLanguage`), the `barre` event (`bar()`, `onBar`: a malformed bar counts as "no bar"), the `ambiance` event (`ambiance()`, `onAmbiance`: the ambiance and colour the person chose for GFS and its apps, read by the kit's `normalizeAmbiance` and followed only by `js/gfs-ambiance.js`; anything that is not an ambiance message counts as "no ambiance") and the `barre.masquer` call with no answer expected (`hideBar`), called only by `js/gfs-bar.js`, `installer`, the `stockage.ecrire` call that keeps a changed preference at GFS (`hasStorage`, `storageGet`, `storageSet`, called only by `js/person-store.js`), and the `ouvrir` call GFS sends (its `{ ouvert }` answer; any other call gets `GFS_INCONNU`; for the `importer` reason it also carries `importation: { valeur, fichier }`, the `File` GFS had the person choose, which `normalizeOpening` keeps for that reason only); `opensInNewTab(href)` says whether a `mailto:` or `tel:` link must open in a new tab (hosted only: in GFS's isolated frame, following it inside the frame would kill the frame). Alone, no message is written or listened to. |
| `js/gfs-bar.js` | GFS's bar as the widget sees it, when GFS shows it over the frame (its edge, depth and zone in the frame's pixels: `GfsLink.bar()` when the context arrives, then the `barre` event): it copies it into `<html data-gfs-bar="left|top|right|bottom">` (absent without a bar) and into eight always-set variables (`--gfs-bar-x`, `-y`, `-width`, `-height`, then `--gfs-bar-inset-left`, `-top`, `-right`, `-bottom`: the bar's room at its own edge only, `0px` elsewhere and without a bar), and gives it to the code through `GfsBar.edge()`, `zone()`, `overlaps(rect)` (strict overlap), `limits(rect)` (the frame, narrowed on the bar's side to the zone's inner edge, only if `rect` crosses it along the edge: enough to cap a size or bound a shift), `apart(rect)` (the shortest shift `{dx, dy}`, perpendicular to the edge, that takes `rect` out of the zone), `settle(rect, anchor, held)` (what an already-placed component that covers the zone must do, `{dx, dy, cap}`: slide sideways, drop or move up, or be capped to the height that remains, without leaving the frame or its anchor; `held` for a list held by its bottom; only `ViewportFit.avoidBar` calls it), `bite(rect)` (the same measure side by side: how many pixels the zone enters a rectangle, by the side at the bar's edge; only one of the four sides is non-zero, which is what a panel along that edge gives up or an element steps back by), `onChange(fn)` and `hide(masked)` (the `barre.masquer` request, which only this file sends). No number is written in the code: the zone comes from GFS, which measured it. Alone, or with a GFS that has no bar: no edge, variables at `0px`, nothing moves. This file moves nothing by itself: the components that use it do. Loaded right after `js/person-store.js`, before any other script. |
| `js/toolbar-gap.js` | The room the toolbar's three top rows leave for GFS's bar, at any edge and in small panels ("Leave a gap", chosen by the owner: top on 21/10, the other edges on 22/10): line by line, the first button the bar's zone really covers (by more than the gap between two lines of its row) jumps to its right edge (a left margin on a single element, class `pp-gfs-gap` and variable `--pp-gfs-gap`, `css/toolbar-v2.css`) or, on the right, steps back (a right margin on itself if it is pushed against the end of its line) or wraps to the next line with what follows it (a right margin on the element before it, class `pp-gfs-room`, variable `--pp-gfs-room`); what follows it follows in order, what is before does not move, the separator before the jumped button follows it; each attempt is measured and undone if it does not clear the zone or changes the button's line; no button is hidden and no band is added at 700×400. A separator (`.bar-sep` of the template and export row, `.v2-sep` of the formatting row) that the row wrap leaves alone at the start or end of a line has its line hidden (class `pp-sep-alone`, `visibility: hidden` in `css/toolbar-v2.css`: its place stays, nothing moves; owner's choice, 22/10, "Hide it when it is alone"), with or without the bar. Recomputed, on rows put back as they are without a bar, when the bar changes (`GfsBar.onChange`), the frame is resized, or a button is shown or hidden. Without a bar, alone or with a GFS from before 025, nothing moves; at the top, in a narrow frame, the template selector shortens (CSS) to end before the zone (120 px at least). Loaded after `js/viewport-fit.js`. |
| `js/gfs-open.js` | What the widget does with an opening GFS asks for: the template GFS designates opens at startup, before the usual order (the row, the view's template, the ★; only the template reopened after a reload the app asked for, `Settings.takeReopenId`, comes before it), and on the `ouvrir` call (through the template list's path, so with its "Save / Discard / Cancel" question when changes are waiting); the status corner says why. "New" (no file, a type) opens instead a blank template of that type, at startup as on the call, through the path of the "New" button of the same type (`openBlankOfType` in `js/main.js`); its "template not saved" warning stays. An opening with the `importer` reason ("New > Import") carries the file GFS had the person choose: it is never a "New" of the type, the value (`excel`, `markdown`, `powerpoint`) names the import of the "+" menu (`importFileFromGfs` in `js/main.js`), which reads that file instead of the picker's, with its sentences, refusals and save question; at startup, it follows the opening of the usual template. Nothing is written to the document or to the settings. |
| `js/gfs-ambiance.js` | GFS's ambiance laid on the app's page (directive 041): in GFS's "Theme" window the person picks an ambiance (Grist Factory, Pop, Papier, Brut, Néon, as GFS names them) and, if they want, its colour, and the editor's bar, menus and windows take it (colours, rounded corners, shadows, fonts, backdrop). `js/gfs-link.js` reads the message; this file loads the kit's posing module (`kit/js/ambiance-pose.js`, never `kit/js/ambiance.js`, which would start a second link in the frame) and hands it `GfsLink`: the nineteen colour tokens of the mode the app is showing and the shape tokens are written on `<html>` through the CSSOM (never a `style` attribute or a stylesheet taken from the message) and are removed on their own when GFS says "no ambiance". `data-gfs-ambiance` and `data-gfs-forme` on `<html>` tell `css/gfs-ambiance.css` what is posed; the other stylesheets read the tokens with their original value as fallback. Hosted only; with the module missing, the app keeps its colours and says so once in the console. The page written in the editor, the PDF, the e-mail and the exports read no token: `dev-tests/ambiance-exceptions.json` says why each colour written in the stylesheets stays there. |
| `js/extensions.js` | The extension registry: each one's files, loaded on first use only and if GFS says it is active, and what a greyed-out feature says on click (GFS opens its installation, or a plain window says it must be used through GFS). |
| `js/pdf-reading.js` | PDF reading as the base of the widget sees it: GFS keeps the PDFs and hands over the one to read (`lecture: { id, fichier }`, `GfsOpen`, at startup and on the `ouvrir` call); the app shows it in its Reading mode. `PdfReading.open` checks that the extension is active and that GFS provides pdf.js, loads the extension on the first PDF only, reads the PDF (an app window asks for the password of a protected one), THEN has the open template left (the "Save / Don't save / Cancel" question if it is modified; an unreadable PDF costs nothing) and answers GFS yes or no; a template that opens closes the PDF and gives back the earlier mode. Meanwhile `js/main.js` greys out what does not apply to a PDF (editing, saving, exporting; comments only for a PDF without text or when Grist refuses the table: `PdfComments.lockReason`), with the reason as a tooltip and on click. "Comment" goes through `PdfReading.comment` when a PDF is on screen. |
| `extensions/lecture-pdf/pdf-engine.js`, `extensions/lecture-pdf/pdf-view.js` (and `pdf-view.css`) | The "PDF reading" extension: the engine opens the PDF with pdf.js, which GFS carries (never a CDN or an `import()`: GFS's file loads through `<script type="module">`, with its fingerprint), page by page; the screen draws the pages one under the other in Reading, at the zoom of its sheets, a page being drawn only when it nears the screen (at most eight stay drawn), with its selectable text layer. The commented passages are highlighted in that layer (`pp-pdf-mark` marks, set on the pieces of text without touching the page), the selection reads as an anchor (`selection`), and the banner of comments without a passage sits at the head of the pages (`setBar`). |
| `extensions/lecture-pdf/pdf-anchor.js` | The anchor of a PDF comment: what it takes to say where the commented passage is and to find it again when the PDF has changed, never with a pixel or a screen coordinate. `pageText` puts pdf.js's `str` end to end (blanks reduced to one space, with where each sign comes from); `anchorFromRange` writes the anchor (the page, the place of the passage in that text, the quote, 24 signs before and after); `locate` finds the passage in a page (where it was, else by its quote, the best surroundings telling two identical passages apart); `relocate` looks for it in the whole PDF (the original page first, then step by step) handing control back to the browser between two pages. One single function writes the anchor and finds it again: a mouse selection and a page's text compare sign by sign, drawn or not. |
| `extensions/lecture-pdf/pdf-comments.js` | The comments of a PDF read in Reading. The threads live in `Publipostage_Commentaires`, keyed by `LectureId` (the reading's `id` that GFS gives, never the file name: a renamed PDF keeps its threads), without `ModeleId`, the anchor and "resolved" on the first message's row; the window is a template's (`Comments.kit`). Opening a PDF writes nothing: the missing columns arrive by `AddColumn` with the first published thread (the table, after the person agrees, if the document has none); Grist refusing the structure leaves "Comment" greyed out for that PDF, with the reason. The threads are read again when the PDF opens, when a thread opens and after each gesture (never continuously). Undo and Redo cover every gesture (`js/action-history.js`), a message being recognized by what it says and never by its row number. A replaced PDF keeps its threads: the passage is looked for at every opening (`PdfAnchor.relocate`, the anchor never rewritten); when it no longer exists the thread stays, listed by a banner at the head of the pages ("N comments without a passage") and readable, resolvable and deletable under "Passage not found". A PDF deleted from GFS leaves its threads in the table: the application purges nothing. |
| `js/live-entry.js` | Live editing as the base of the widget sees it: its row of the "Collaborative editing" menu greyed out without the extension (never hidden: the reason as a tooltip, and a click explains or opens GFS's installation), the activation (the extension's code and GFS's file load only on the first click, or when a live room is seen on the open template), the automatic entry (when one person turns live writing on, everyone who has the same template open - already there or arriving later - joins on their own: the base re-reads the `Publipostage_Direct` table every 3 s, only when the extension is active and nothing on screen can be lost), and the single entry point through which the rest of the widget reaches it: `isLive` (the header and footer, track changes, linked tables and the version history close), `undo` and `redo` per person, `blocksAutosave` and the save token (one person saves the template for everyone), `places` and `onPeers` (where the others are: the slide strip draws the dots under the thumbnails from them). |
| `js/slides-entry.js` | Presentations as the base of the widget sees them: the "presentation" type (declared to `js/templates.js` with the extension's name: it opens when GFS has turned on the "diapositives" extension, otherwise it stays greyed out in the lists, never lost), a presentation's page (the 16:9 slide in landscape, `PageLayout.SLIDE_FORMAT`, for a blank presentation or one without its own format), the "New presentation" row of the "+" menu greyed out without the extension (a click explains or opens GFS's installation), and the single entry point through which the rest of the widget reaches them: the slide strip (shown while a presentation is on screen in Edit mode, its code loaded only when the extension is active; turned off along the way, the strip stays and its actions are greyed out) and the page of every slide, the last one included even when empty (`showsEveryPage` for the editor's pagination, and for the PDF, which then reports no blank page; `outputHtml`, which marks a presentation's page breaks, for Reading and exports); `status`, the status corner where the strip writes "Slide 3 copied." (set by `js/main.js`); a slide's new text, at 20 pt, and its larger headings (44, 36, 28 pt…), set as a mark like a size chosen from the menu (`createTextExtension`, wired by `js/editor.js`: the pending marks of an empty line, pasted plain text, a block that changes type; `textMarks` for the layouts of "Add"). Towards a document: `registerTextImport` and `openTextImport` (the "Slide from a presentation…" row of the "Link and content blocks" menu, greyed out in an e-mail and in a presentation, which calls the extension's `ensureFor`), `pastedInDocument` (a slide copied from the strip and pasted into the text becomes text of the document, called by `transformPastedHTML` of `js/editor.js`, and the status line says so) `outputHtml` keeps its last three results (an HTML of more than 20,000 characters, never when a slide is linked to another: its source is read again each time), since Reading asks for it at every entry; when it is redone, it reads the HTML only once for all its steps (`SlideProps.forOutputTree`, then `FreeFrame.withoutHiddenIn`, `SlideTheme.forOutputIn` and `markSlideBreaks` on the same tree; the text-based `forOutput` and `withoutHidden` functions remain for the PowerPoint export, `keepProps`, and other callers). |
| `js/slide-text.js` | A slide as text of a document, the base (`SlideText`): `toDocument` converts a slide's HTML (read in an inert document, `HtmlSanitize.parseInert`) into blocks of the document - the running text first, then the text of each free frame in reading order (top to bottom, within 12 pt, then left to right) -, without the slide's sizes unless "Keep the original formatting" is checked, with the images in front of or behind the text put into the text; a shape keeps its text, a line, the speaker notes, the transition and the background are counted and said (`report`), the marker of a linked slide never goes through; `pasted` reads the paste of a slide (the clipboard's `data-pp-slide` envelope, several slides without the page break that separated them); `leaveBlockAtEnd` puts the cursor after the text. The base keeps it on its own: a document that receives a slide through Ctrl+V converts it without loading the extension. `toSlide` does the reverse for a document page: its sizes removed then a slide's put in (20 pt, headings at 44, 36, 28 pt), or kept by "Keep the original formatting"; `isBlank` tells whether a block is empty |
| `js/slide-copies.js` (and `css/slide-copies.css`) | A slide's text linked to the slide, in a document, the base (`SlideCopies`): the copy is the top-level blocks that `js/slide-text.js` places, followed by the `slideLink` marker of `js/slide-links.js` (zero height, never visible), which says the slide (`data-link-model`, `-key`, `-slide`), "Follows the source" (`data-link-mode="pull"`), the number of blocks before it (`data-link-span`), whether the original formatting is kept (`data-link-keep`) and the base (`data-link-base`); `copiesOf` reads them (once per text), `insert` places one (one Ctrl+Z, never inside another copy), `follow` updates them when the document opens and at each autosave read (a transaction outside the history that does not make the document "to save", `SlideLinks.markSync`), `createExtension` refuses whatever would change a copy's text while its presentation exists (typing, pasting, commands; a gesture that takes the whole copy away with its marker goes through) and draws the accent line (`pp-copy-mark`), `statusAt` and `detach` serve the floating bar of `js/floating-toolbars.js`, `forOutput` gives the outputs (Reading, PDF, Word, e-mails, macro-templates) the text without the marker, taken from the slide when it has changed |
| `js/page-copies.js` | A document page's slides, linked to the page, the base (`PageCopies`): the page is what lies between two top-level page breaks, found again by the `slideLink` marker (`data-slide-id`) that `anchorPage` writes into the source document's HTML (`withMarker`: at the end of the page, at the start of the last one; `Templates.saveContent`, the page read again beforehand); the slides carry `data-link-page` ("model:token:page:rank:fingerprint:formatting", `parse`, `write`; `groupsOf`: all those of the same page make a group); `planOf` compares the page's fingerprint (`pageHash`) with the one of the last sync, `follow` takes a changed group again in one transaction outside the history (`pullOf`: in place when the number of slides has not changed, otherwise rebuilt where the first one was; `convertPage` goes through `PageSlides.fromPage`, which may not be loaded: `afterExtension`), `refuses` is the lock (a `filterTransaction`), `detach` frees the group, `infoOf` says the badge and the menu; in the source document, `sparesMarkers` refuses a transaction that would only remove a page marker and `through` (Backspace, Delete) goes past it; no library. |
| `js/slide-links.js` | A presentation's linked slides, the base (`SlideLinks`): the hidden marker at the end of a slide imported linked (the `slideLink` node of `js/editor-nodes.js`, `<div class="pp-slide-link">` in the saved HTML: the source presentation, its slide, the base of the last sync, the direction); the sync on opening and at each read of the autosave (`follow`, outside the history), the sending to the source in the same batch as the presentation (`prepareSend`, `alsoWrite` of `Templates.save`), the source page taking what reaches it (`adopt`); the safeguards (a paste or a copy never links, an erased marker comes back, a locked slide refuses typing); outputs without the marker (`forOutput`). The interface plugs in through `wire`. No library. |
| `js/free-frame.js` (and `css/free-frame.css`) | A presentation's free text frames, the base (`FreeFrame`): the `freeFrame` node (a top-level block that holds text, kept at the start of its slide; rendered as `div.pp-free-frame`, of zero height, whose box floats at its measures - points from the page corner, minus the margins and the band the header reserves, `--pp-top-band`, set by the editor and by Reading - without ever taking room in the text or in the pagination), putting one in the middle of the cursor's slide (`insert`), the mouse gestures (the edges move, the eight handles resize, a single Ctrl+Z per gesture, Esc abandons it; `gesture`, `storedHeight`, `keepReachable`), the keyboard (arrows one point, Shift ten, Enter, Esc), the floating bar (the fill, the line, its weight and its style, the transparency of both, "Order" (bring to front, send to back, "Behind text": one button and its menu), duplicate, delete; `wireToolbar`) and the tidying (`tidyTransaction`: a frame that ends up in the middle of a text, through a paste, goes back up to the start of its slide; outside a presentation it becomes text again; never when a template loads). A frame's height is a minimum: it grows with its text. A frame overflows its slide when the rectangle that wraps its box, rotated and at the height its text gives it, leaves the page (`overflowsPage`; the CSS clips it there): the strip says so with a red dot. A frame can be a SHAPE (its kind `shape`, its fill `fill`, the colour `line` and weight `lw` of its line): the node writes the variables that `css/free-frame.css` reads to draw it (`--ff-fill`, `--ff-clip`, `--ff-ring`...), without a script. The fill and the line can be TRANSLUCENT (`fillTr` and `lineTr`, integers from 0, opaque, to 100, invisible; reset to 0 when there is no fill or no line; written `data-ff-fill-tr` and `data-ff-line-tr` only when they are not zero, so a bare frame does not change): the "Transparency" slider at the foot of the bar's color menus (`transparencyRow`, `wireTransparency`: `input` shows the gesture through `paint` without touching the document, `change` puts it in a single history step through `patch` and the menu stays open; Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y there are the Undo and Redo buttons) sets them, and the CSS reads them through `--ff-fill-op` (the fill, `::before`), `--ff-line-op` (the line, `::after`; a line's ink is its `::before`) and `--ff-fill-inset` (the fill stops at the MIDDLE of a translucent line, as in the PDF, Word and PowerPoint, where the line is centred on the outline). The line can be DASHED or have ROUND or SQUARE caps (`dash`: solid, one of PowerPoint's ten patterns - `sysDot`, `dot`, `sysDash`, `dash`, `lgDash`, `sysDashDot`, `dashDot`, `lgDashDot`, `sysDashDotDot`, `lgDashDotDot` - or a list of at most eight "dash:gap" pairs, in line weights, `dashOf`; `cap`: flat, `round` or `square`): written `data-ff-dash` and `data-ff-cap` only when they are not empty, reset to empty when there is no line (`normalized`), set by the bar's "Line style" menu (`dashMenu`, `markDash`: eleven patterns in a grid and three caps, greyed with their reason while nothing shows them; a choice is a single history step, and a dash chosen for an object without a line gives it a new shape's line). Neither the CSS border nor the solid line's ring can make dashes: the node then writes the line's SVG IMAGE (`ShapeGeometry.strokeArt`, `lineArt`) in `--ff-art` (stretched to the box: ellipse, polygons, line) or `--ff-art-nine` (a nine-slice border image, cut at `--ff-slice`, with the thickness `--ff-edge`: a rectangle, a rounded rectangle or a text frame keeps its corners and its line's thickness when it grows with its text, but the dashes of its vertical sides grow longer with it), which the `::after` layer paints from the stylesheet alone, without a script; `HtmlSanitize.clean` lets only that image through (`ART_PROPS`, `isSafeArt`: a `data:image/svg+xml` that holds only `path` elements with known attributes). A solid, simple line with flat caps keeps its former drawing. The line can also be COMPOUND (`cmpd`: `dbl` double, `thinThick` thin then thick, `thickThin` thick then thin, `tri` triple; `cmpdOf`): parallel bands that together make up the line's weight, from the outer edge inwards (`ShapeGeometry.compoundBands`: three thirds for the double, quarters for the two others, sixths for the triple; PowerPoint does not document these proportions, they are approximate). Written as `data-ff-cmpd` only when not empty, reset to empty without a line color (`normalized`), set by the row of five types in the "Line style" menu (`cmpdSample`, `markDash`). The line's image then holds one `path` per band (`strokeArt` insets the outline to the middle of each band, `outlineInset`; `lineParts` shifts each band sideways, the first one to the left of the line's direction), with the dashes and the cap of each band: the pattern follows the weight of the whole line and the cap that of the band; the dashes of a line's bands are aligned, those of a shape's bands, each drawn along its own outline, are not aligned with one another. The exports: the PDF paints a frame behind the text at the back of the page and a frame in front of the text in its page's footer, which pdfmake paints after all the content (`visitFrame`, `placeFrames`, `frontFramesAt`, `liftFrontImages`, `frameDrawingFrom` of `js/pdf-export.js`: a vector canvas), Word makes them text boxes or preset shapes anchored to the page, behind or in front of the text (`frameShapeFrom`, `presetShapeProperties`, `frameLayerOf` of `js/docx-export.js`), PowerPoint makes them the PowerPoint preset shapes that carry their name, in place, rotated and in the order of their layer (`frameXml` of `js/pptx-export.js`), HTML and Markdown keep only their text; the transparency becomes `fillOpacity` and `strokeOpacity` in the PDF (`frameVectorsOf`, `lineDrawingFrom`, and `fill-opacity` and `stroke-opacity` of a rotated frame's SVG, `vectorSvg`; nothing is painted at 100%) and `a:alpha` in the color of Word (`fadeFill`) and PowerPoint (`colorXml`); the dash and the cap become the `dash` (with its phase, `dashPhase`) and the `lineCap` of the PDF's canvas (`frameVectorsOf`, `lineDrawingFrom`: a line with round caps or a compound one is drawn piece by piece, one band after the other, `lineParts`) and `stroke-dasharray`, `stroke-linecap` and `stroke-dashoffset` of a rotated frame's SVG (`vectorSvg`), `a:prstDash` (or `a:custDash` for a list) and `cap` of the line in Word (`dashElementOf`, `dashStroke`) and PowerPoint (`dashXml`, `lineXml`); the compound line becomes one vector per band in the PDF, `compoundLine` (`cmpd`) in Word and `cmpd` in PowerPoint. A frame, a shape or a line is IN FRONT of the slide's text (the original setting, no attribute) or BEHIND it (`layer` = `behind`, written `data-ff-layer`; the anchor takes the z-index -1 of `css/free-frame.css`, inside the editor's stacking context: under the text, on the paper): the "Behind text" row of the bar's "Order" menu flips it in a single history step, like an image's. The slide's text covers the whole width of its column and would receive every click: `reachBehind` (a `mousemove` of the editor) therefore gives the clicks back to the frame behind where the pointer is on no letter (`reachesBehind`, `onTextLine`: the `pp-ff-reaching` class of the editor's host takes the clicks away from the text, never during a gesture; the rule of `css/free-frame.css` targets the text by the `pp-ff-flow` class, which the `freeFrameFlow` plugin - a decoration - puts on its blocks while a frame is behind the text: a subject without a class, "everything that is not a frame", made every frame restyle at each toggle) and lets the chosen frame's edges and handles be grabbed even under a letter. A LINE is a second node, `freeLine` (an atomic top-level block, without text, put away and counted like a frame by `isFrame`: `tidyTransaction`, `insert`, pasting, the keyboard and the toolbar treat it alike; the same `div.pp-free-frame` rendering, its kind `line`): its box is that of its two ends (the width or the height may be zero), its direction `flip` (`h`, `v`, `hv`: PowerPoint's `flipH` and `flipV`), its arrowheads `head` (at the start) and `tail` (at the end: `triangle`, `arrow`, `stealth`, `diamond` or `oval`) with their width and length (`headW`, `headLen`, `tailW`, `tailLen`: `sm`, `lg`, empty for the medium, never without an arrowhead), its color `line` and weight `lw`; its DOM box overflows what the ink takes (`--ff-bleed`) and receives no click: only the band that follows the stroke (`--ff-grab`, 9 points at least) and its two ends (`--ff-sx`…`--ff-ey`) can be grabbed, so the text underneath stays reachable; its gestures are `lineGesture` (move, pull an end, Shift: 15° angles), its placing `insertLine`, its view `lineView`, and its toolbar has the ends in place of the fill (`ENDS`: four icons that say which ends carry an arrow, then, for each end, six shapes and nine sizes drawn with the end's shape, `endsMenuHtml`; a size can only be picked for an end that has an arrowhead). It has only its drawing: the PDF makes it a vector polygon (`lineDrawingFrom`), Word the flipped `line` preset with `a:headEnd` and `a:tailEnd` (`type`, `w`, `len`: `lineShapeFrom`), HTML drops it (`rootOf`), Markdown keeps nothing of it. A frame or a shape can ROTATE (`rot`, degrees clockwise, brought into [0, 360[ to the hundredth by `turnOf`, always 0 for a line; written `data-ff-rot` and `--ff-rot`, no attribute when the frame is upright): the box turns around its center (`transform: rotate` in `css/free-frame.css`, and the ANCHOR then clips to the page), its measures stay those of its upright box (`straight` measures a box without its rotation); the round handle above it (`.pp-ff-rotate`, `startRotation`: one history step, Shift holds 15°, Esc abandons, the angle is read in the `.pp-ff-angle` badge) and Alt+← → (`turnBy`: 15°, with Shift 1°) rotate it, `rotationAt` gives a gesture's angle and `cursorFor` the cursor of the side handles. The PDF, whose pdfmake rotates nothing, draws a rotated frame as SVG (`rotatedFrameFrom`, `frameTextSvg`, `vectorSvg`, `svgImage` of `js/pdf-export.js`: its shape, its words one by one, its links, its images), Word writes `a:xfrm rot` (`transformation.rotation` in `frameShapeFrom`), HTML and Markdown keep nothing of it. A slide's background is drawn there twice: in the editor by its marker (`.pp-slide-props[data-bg]::before`, under the text and under the frames), in the outputs by a full-page frame (`.pp-free-frame[data-ff-bg]`, `backgroundHtml`) that the PDF paints at the back of the page (`frameFrom`) and Word puts behind the text (`frameShapeFrom`); HTML removes it (`rootOf`). The "Line style" menu greys out the dash or compound type that would push a shape's drawing past what Reading keeps (`artFits`, `lookRefused`: 30,000 characters). No library. The text columns (`cols` from 2 to 16, `colGap` in points, 18 by default; `columnsOf`, `columnGapOf`, `columnVars`) set the text in columns through the CSS (`--ff-cols`, `--ff-col-gap`); the "Text columns" button of the bar opens a menu of one to four columns (`columnsMenu`, `markColumns`), a PowerPoint file's own number is added as a last row; the PDF draws them like a rotated frame (`rotatedFrameFrom`), Word and PowerPoint write them as `numCol` and `spcCol`. The turned text (`vert` 'vert' or 'vert270', `verticalOf`) is written with a vertical `writing-mode` (CSS, `data-ff-vert` attribute); the "Text direction" button (`orientMenu`, `markOrient`) sets it; `horizontal` and `flatten` lay the frame flat (width and height swapped, margins turned) while an export reads its text: the PDF draws it turned by ±90° about the centre, Word and PowerPoint write `vert`. The bar controls that change the object - the "Bring to front" / "Send to back" rows of the "Order" menu (`reorder`), "Duplicate" (`duplicate`), "Delete" (`remove`) and the fill or line of a color menu (`patch`) - are each ONE editor transaction (`own`, never `addToHistory: false`): ONE Ctrl+Z (Ctrl+Y) undoes (redoes) them and the object's selection, hence its bar, follows; the `undoFramesMouse` script tests it. A frame at rest has only its `.pp-ff-ui` layer and its four edges as interface (`buildUi`); the eight handles, the rotation and the link mark (`addHandles`, built together by `ensureHandles` of the node view) are built on first need and never removed again: a frame that is chosen (`selectNode`), active or in a multiple selection (the `pp-ff-active` / `pp-ff-selected` decorations, read in `update` and, for a frame born with the cursor inside it, in the `decorations` of its creation: `showsHandles`), or linked (`a.link`, the mark shows without a choice) - 5,700 fewer elements in "Sales presentation" (494 frames); the saved HTML and the CSS are unchanged. The frame's alternative text (`alt` / `data-ff-alt`, `altOf`: its name for screen readers) and the object's hiding (`hidden` / `data-ff-hidden`, the "Hide object" button) are two more attributes: `withoutHiddenIn` removes the hidden objects from a tree already read (`withoutHidden` is the same on a text). |
| `js/slide-links-live.js` | "On typing" (`SlideLinksLive`): when the "Collaborative editing" extension is active and a presentation with shared slides is on screen, two open presentations follow each other while typing, through the live table (`Publipostage_Direct`, one row per page and per channel: `Doc` = `slides:N`, `Kind` = `l`, `Data` = the changed slides, their large images as `pp-media:` references). Each page's presence (nothing is sent while no other presentation is there), sending `SEND_MS` after a keystroke, reading at a variable pace (never with the page hidden or the app inactive), the arrived version set outside the history by the base, the cleanup on leaving. Live writing's rows are never read here, nor these by it. No library. |
| `js/frame-anim.js` | A slide object's animation, the core (`FrameAnim`; job 8c of the PowerPoint import): the setting of a text frame, shape or free line (`data-ff-anim="fly:l out with d700 w200 n3"`: effect appear, fade, fly, wipe or zoom, side, entrance or exit, start on click, with or after previous, duration, delay, order), its text and bounds (`normalized`, `text`, `parse`), a slide's steps (`stepsOf`: step 0 starts by itself, one step per click, "with" and "after" counted), the movements (`keyframes`, `play`: on the object's box through `translate`, `scale`, `opacity` and a mask, never `transform`, which its rotation uses) and the slide's rank stamped on each animated object for the slide show (`stampSlides`, `framesOf`). Nothing moves in the editor or in the outputs. |
| `js/frame-link.js` | A slide object's link, the core (`FrameLink`): the setting of a frame or shape (`data-ff-link`: a `#slide-3` bookmark, a web, mail or phone address, or a slide show action - `pp:next`, `pp:prev`, `pp:first`, `pp:last`, `pp:back`, `pp:end`), its text and what it refuses (`text`, `parse`: read again at every use), the linked object under a screen point (`frameAt`, for the slide show - the separate window's too, which loads this file - and for Read mode), opening an address (`openUrl`, the text link's too), the link's name for a tooltip (`describe`), the animation the link plays in place of the target slide's transition (`data-ff-link-trans`, "effect side duration" or `none`: `canAnimate` says whether the link leads to a slide, `transText` reads it again at every use through `SlideProps.normalized`, `transOf` hands it to the player). The slide show (`extensions/diapositives/slide-player.js`: `followFrame`, the last slide viewed), Read mode (`js/reader-mode.js`: `followFrameLink`) and the "Link…" window (`js/link-dialog.js`: `openForObject`, the "Slide show actions" group) use it; nothing shows in the document, the PDF or the Word. |
| `js/slide-view.js` (and `css/free-frame.css`, the `.pp-ff-view` block) | A slide's view, the core (`SlideView`; requested on 02/11, "Link, then animated preview in a row", third level, second part): a free frame with `data-ff-view="<anchor>"` (`FreeFrame`: `view`, `insertView`, `normalized` - an address of 64 characters at most, never on a line) that shows another slide of the template, small and live. The saved text carries only the frame, its link (`data-ff-link`, hence the `data-ff-link-trans` animation) and a fallback text - the shown slide's title, in a white frame with a thin grey line - which the PDF, Word, HTML and PowerPoint (with the object link) show; the view itself is never saved.
