The editor: the frame's node view puts down a layer (`host`, `.pp-ff-view`) and declares it (`track`); `redraw` fills it with the copy the strip makes for its thumbnails (`SlideStrip.pageCopy`), reduced to the frame's box (`fit`: the `--ffv-*` variables, centred, never distorted), 250 ms after each change of the text (`schedule`, called by the `freeFrameViews` plugin of `js/free-frame.js`, and by the strip when the theme changes: the copy carries the theme's decor of the shown slide, at its rank, after its marker: `withDecor`) and when a chart finishes painting; a slide whose blocks have not changed is not copied again; the strip then redraws the thumbnails of the slides that carry a view (`SlideStrip.redrawViews`). Without the strip, or when the shown slide no longer exists, the layer hides and the fallback text reads. The copy is inert; only the layer takes the mouse (a click selects the frame, a drag moves it). Read mode, printing and the slide show: `fill(wrapper)`, called by `ReaderMode.resolveReaderBody`, copies the shown slide's blocks into the already resolved HTML (`blocksOf`); the slide show asks for the anchors only of a text that has a view (`shows`). A view never shows a view (`tidy`). The "Objects" menu: "Slide view…" (`openPicker`, the searchable list of slides; `insert` puts the view down with the chosen slide's anchor, a new one if it has none, in a single Ctrl+Z). No library. |
| `extensions/diapositives/frame-anim-ui.js` (and `frame-anim.css`) | An object's "Animation…" window (opened by the button of its toolbar, `js/free-frame.js`): a radio-button draft (effect, type, side, start, duration, delay) on the common base of the "Transition…" window, a preview that replays the effect through `FrameAnim.play`, groups that do not apply greyed with their reason, "Apply" in one history step. |
| `js/shape-geometry.js` | The drawing of shapes (`ShapeGeometry`), in points from the box's top left corner, without touching the page: the sixteen kinds (`rect`, `roundRect`, `ellipse`, `triangle`, `diamond`, `rightArrow`, `leftArrow`, `star5`, `upArrow`, `downArrow`, `chevron`, `pentagon`, `hexagon`, `parallelogram`, `plus`, `wedgeRoundRectCallout` - PowerPoint's preset shape names, with their outlines and text rectangles at the original settings; the speech bubble has its tail inside its box, `BUBBLE_BODY`), their original size (`defaults`), their outline (`outline`: a rectangle, a rounded rectangle, an ellipse or a polygon), the outline pulled in by the line weight (`inset`, `strokeOutline`), what the CSS reads from it (`look`: the rounding, the border, the polygon in percentages, the line's ring, and, when the line is translucent, how far the fill is pulled in to stop at the middle of the line: `fillInset`, `fillRadius`), each kind's text margins (`textInsets`) and the weight the box can carry (`lineWidthFor`). The DASHED line, or the one with round caps, is drawn here too: `DASHES` (PowerPoint's ten patterns), `dashOf` and `dashPairs` (a pattern or a list of "dash:gap" pairs read and brought to its canonical form; a list equal to a pattern takes its name), `capOf`, `dashArray` (the lengths in points for SVG and PDF, one weight shorter with a round or square cap, which lengthens each dash), `dashPhase` (the PDF's offset), `dashDrawingMl` (the pattern in DrawingML for Word and PowerPoint) and the line's IMAGE, an SVG of `path` elements only (`strokeArt` for a shape or a text frame, cut in nine for a rectangle; `lineParts` and `lineArt` for a line: the dashed body, then the solid arrowheads). The COMPOUND line has its bands here (`COMPOUNDS`, `cmpdOf`, `compoundBands`: from the outer edge inwards, as fractions of the weight) and its outline inset by the wanted distance (`outlineInset`). The LINE (kind `line`, 192 × 0 points) has its own drawing: `lineOutline` (the one-piece polygon of the stroke's body and its arrowheads when they are solid triangles, two, three or five times the weight - two points at least - by size, in the frame of its two ends' box; `lineIsPlain` says when that polygon is enough: solid, simple line with flat caps and triangular arrowheads), `lineLook` (what the CSS reads from it: the margin `bleed` that the ink takes outside the box, the ink polygon, that of the band you grab, the two ends), `lineEnds`, `flipOf`, `endOf` and `endSizeOf` (the direction, the arrowheads and their size as read: an unknown value is read as the nearest one). PowerPoint's FIVE ARROWHEADS have their drawing here (`END_TYPES`, `END_SIZES`, `endShape`, `endCap`: the solid triangle, the open arrow `arrow` made of two V strokes, the notched arrow `stealth` hollowed at six tenths of its length, the diamond and the oval, those two centred on the line's end and the oval drawn with forty sides), each in three widths and three lengths; a line shorter than its arrowheads shrinks them together. The editor (`css/free-frame.css`), the PDF and Word read the same outlines there. Any OTHER PowerPoint shape (batch 4a of "PowerPoint import without loss", 23/10), and one of the sixteen when it carries settings (`drawnByPresets`), is computed by `js/shape-presets.js`: `presetBox` fits its geometry inside the frame ("the box is the ink": the frame holds the stroke, the mitred points of its corners and whatever comes out of the shape; `geo`, a file's kept geometry, is used as long as it fills the frame exactly), `presetFrame` is its inverse (the import gets the frame from it), `presetPlace` the geometry's box on the page (the `xfrm` of Word and PowerPoint, rotation included), `presetSvg` and `presetArt` the drawing of the fill and the stroke (two `data:` images the CSS paints, paths the PDF takes over), `presetIcon` the menu icon; `TABS` sorts the menu's 64 shapes into four tabs of sixteen. No library. |
| `js/shape-presets.js` (and `js/shape-presets-data.js`, `dev-tests/build-shape-presets.py`) | PowerPoint's 187 PRESET shapes (`ShapePresets`), computed as PowerPoint does from the standard's file that defines them (presetShapeDefinitions.xml, ECMA-376 part 1): `js/shape-presets-data.js` keeps their definitions as compact data (the default settings, the guides, the paths, the text rectangle; data only, produced by `dev-tests/build-shape-presets.py` from the standard's file), this module derives from them, for a box of `w` by `h` points and settings `adj` (the yellow handles; `adjOf`, `adjText`: only those the shape knows, integers, different from the default), the paths (`paths`: the fill, the lightened or darkened faces, the stroke; arcs as Bézier curves), their polygons (`flatten`), the ink (`inkBox`), the stroke's ink (`strokeBox`: straddling the outline, mitred corners up to `MITER_LIMIT` = 8 weights, round or square ends), everything the shape covers (`reach`), the geometry that fills a given frame (`fit`: a secant per axis, falling back to the largest that fits, centred; never any ink outside the frame) and the text rectangle (`textRect`). It touches no element of the page; an unknown shape has no path, an impossible computation is 0. No library. FREEFORMS (`a:custGeom`): `freeze(def, w, h, flip)` freezes a file's geometry into a shape NAME (`cust:` and its paths), `isCustom`, `customParts` and `customTree` (for the exports); `has` only reads what the strict grammar of `parseCustom` accepts. |

The button is named after the cursor's slide; greyed out, never removed, outside a presentation ("Objects go on a slide") or with the extension off (its reason, its click explains it): its menu then still opens to say why. The shapes are sorted into four tabs ("Common", the sixteen from before; "Basic"; "Arrows"; "Stars and bubbles": `ShapeGeometry.TABS`, `menuitemradio`s in a group named "Shapes"); a clicked tab shows its grid without closing the menu or taking the keyboard, and the menu keeps its size. | `extensions/diapositives/slide-strip.js` (and `slide-strip.css`) | The "diapositives" (slides) extension: the strip to the left of a presentation, one thumbnail per slide (a copy of the editor's rendering, scaled down to 80 pixels wide), the cursor's one checked; the notes for one slide at a time, the row is greyed out otherwise, `unavailable`), "Add" at the bottom of the strip - the first of the two square one-icon buttons that a row (`actionsRow`) puts on a single line there, with "Import", their name in the tooltip -, which opens, to the right of the strip, the layout menu (`LAYOUTS`, `layoutNodes`: headings, a list, a two-column zone, already written in the interface language, the title's word selected); a thumbnail can be dragged with the mouse (`onDragPress`: a copy follows the mouse, a line shows the target gap, the strip scrolls near its edges, Esc cancels; the `moveTo` action, like "Move up" and "Move down", reorders the slides in a single replacement, `arrange`); several slides chosen together (`picked`, `pick`, `togglePick`: Ctrl+click, Shift+click, Shift+↑ ↓, Ctrl+A; `planOf` turns each action into a new order of the chosen slides, `arrange` applies it in a single replacement, and `slideForClipboard` copies the choice); each action is a single editor transaction (one Ctrl+Z undoes it); the red dot of a slide that overflows its page (read from the editor's pagination) or whose frame leaves the page (`FreeFrame.overflowsPage`), its tooltip and the thumbnail's name saying which (`syncOverflow`, `overflowWhy`); a thumbnail's notes badge (`syncNotes`, `excerptOf`: the start of the notes as a tooltip and in the thumbnail's name); thumbnails are only copied for the rows the strip shows, or nearly (an `IntersectionObserver` on the list), and a row follows its slide when it changes place (`matchRows`); a layered picture (in front of or behind the text) follows its slide when the slide changes page: within the action's transaction (`predictLayers`), then after each pagination (`followLayers`, grouped in the history of the change that caused it), its page read from `HeaderFooterPreview.pageBodyTops`; copying and pasting a slide (Ctrl+C, Ctrl+V, through the browser's `copy` and `paste` events, from one presentation and one tab to another): `onCopy` and `slideForClipboard` (its HTML, without the picture on all pages or the comments, in a `data-pp-slide` wrapper that gives its first page), `onPaste` and `pasteAfter` (one more slide, filled by the editor's paste, one Ctrl+Z), `placePastedLayers` (its layered pictures on its new page); "Import" at the bottom of the strip, which opens the panel of `slide-import.js`, and `importAfterCurrent` (the imported slide, placed like a paste, after the cursor's one); the stylesheet hides a presentation's page breaks in the editor, except the selected one (its place stays). No library. GFS's bar on the left enters it: the strip widens by what the bar bites (`GfsBar.bite`, `--pp-slide-strip-bar`) and the page leaves it the same room. The "Hide slide" row of the ⋯ menu, the pill (crossed-out eye) and the dimmed thumbnail of a hidden slide (`syncHidden`). The people of the live session: under the thumbnail of the slide where another person has their selection, a dot of their color (`syncPeople`, called again by `refresh` and by `onPeers` when `LiveEntry.onPeers` says they arrive, leave or move; the place comes from `LiveEntry.places`), their name as a tooltip and in the thumbnail's name (`labelRows`), four at most then a "+N", sitting across the bottom edge of the thumbnail, outside the layout. |

No library. GFS's bar on the right enters it: the panel widens by what the bar bites (`--pp-slide-import-bar`), the page and the zoom pill leave it the same room. The same panel also puts a slide into a document as text ("text" mode: `openText`, `insertText`, `syncScreen`): every presentation of the document is a source, the thumbnails take the presentation's page, the "Keep the original formatting" box is unchecked at each opening and the source is remembered for each use (`remembered`); a click converts the slide with `SlideText` (`js/slide-text.js`) and puts it after the cursor's paragraph (one Ctrl+Z). A second use: the list of sources also offers the template's documents, a page is a thumbnail on the document's page (its size, orientation and margins, `ownPage`), the box becomes "Keep the original formatting" and a click puts it in as slides after the cursor's one through `PageSlides.fromPage` (`importPage`, one Ctrl+Z, the cursor on the last one placed); the "Keep linked" box comes back above it (`importLinkedPage`: `PageCopies.anchorPage` writes the page's marker into the document, then `SlideStrip.importAfterCurrent` places the slides and `PageCopies.tagImported` gives them their marker, in one Ctrl+Z; a page already linked here, or an empty one, arrives as a plain copy); `keepsFormat` tells the strip's Ctrl+V the box. In text, the "Keep linked" box (the first one, unchecked at each opening) places the slide with its marker (`insertLinkedText`: `SlideLinks.anchorSource` gives the slide its number in its source, `SlideCopies.insert` places the linked text; a slide already linked to the document, or an empty one, arrives as a plain copy) |
| `extensions/diapositives/page-slides.js` | A document page as slides (`PageSlides`): `fromPage` converts the blocks of a page (what lies between two top-level page breaks; a selection that crosses some is taken page by page) into text slides, separated by a page break - a slide's sizes (`SlideText.toSlide`), except "Keep the original formatting" -, and cuts a page that is too long where the editor would cut: the text is laid outside the editor, in a hidden `.tiptap` as wide as the destination slide, and `HeaderFooterPreview.breaksOf` gives the cuts (between two blocks; between two rows of a table, which `tableParts` splits into tables with the same columns; never inside a two-column zone); `fromEditorCopy` and `isEditorCopy` recognize and convert a selection copied from the editor (`data-pm-slice`, without a slide's `data-pp-slide` envelope) for the strip's Ctrl+V. Everything happens on an inert document (`HtmlSanitize.parseInert`); only the copy being measured enters the page. |
| `extensions/diapositives/slide-links-ui.js` | What a person sees of linked slides: the "Ask" window (a slide changed on both sides, its two versions as thumbnails through `SlideImport.paint`: "Keep this one", "Take the other one", "Later", the disagreements one after another) and the link rows of a thumbnail's ⋯ menu (the direction, "Compare both versions", "Detach"; `act`). The "Keep linked" box is in `slide-import.js`, the thumbnails' badges in `slide-strip.js` (`syncLinks`). Styles: `slide-import.css`. No library. |
| `extensions/diapositives/slide-show.js` (and `slide-show.css`) | "Present", a presentation's slide show: the ▶ button to the left of the zoom pill (placed on its line and following it when it moves or opens; invisible until it is there), F5 and Shift+F5, and its ▾ menu to its left ("From slide N", "From the beginning", the "Full screen" switch that the ▶, F5 and Shift+F5 follow; never greyed out: when the frame refuses the browser's full screen - Grist's, GFS's isolated sub-frame -, a line under it says the slide show opens in a separate window -, and the "Presenter view" switch, unticked at the start and kept as long as the page stays open). It chooses where the slide show plays (`routeOf`): the frame allows full screen, the panel with full screen requested in the gesture that starts it; the frame refuses it and the switch is ticked, the separate window (`SlideWindow.open`, in the gesture, right after the question about images from another site; refused by the browser, the panel takes over with a line that says so); the switch unticked, the panel without asking for anything; with the presenter view ticked, a real window (`SPEAKER_WINDOW`, the only one the browser grants for the gesture) plays the slide show and the panel becomes the console (`playWithSpeaker`: `SlideSpeaker`, the copy of the rendering that shows the next slide through `SlideWindow.duplicate`, the titles, notes and hidden slides read from the editor at the start by `speakerSlides`, none of which ever goes to the window; the window is told the console drives it (`windowInfo(true)`: `speaker`, hence its black and white screens and the pen's drawing), receives the pen's points through `onCommand(name, index, points)` and, when reloaded, makes the console's drawing start from zero again: `link.follow(…, …, () => speaker.reset())`); refused, the panel plays the slide show with a dedicated line, without a console. `slide-show.css` also holds the black or white screen's veil (`.pp-slide-show-screen`, in front of the bar and the messages). Nothing is written. No library. | `extensions/diapositives/slide-player.js` | The slide show's player, shared by the panel and the separate window (`SlidePlayer.create`: each player's state is in its own closure): a page-sized sheet, centered on the black screen and enlarged to its size, shows one window at a time (the print windows: a slide that overflows takes several); the keys, the click, the wheel, the bottom bar, the end screen, Esc; full screen (`enterFullscreen`, true when the browser grants it, requested in the person's gesture); an information line (`note`); `onGesture`, through which the separate window takes the first gesture. The slide show skips hidden slides (`visibleFrom`) and moves on by itself after the slide's time (`advanceTimer`). The object animations (`FrameAnim`): an object that enters is hidden on arrival, the animation that starts by itself plays after the transition, each "next" plays a step before moving to the next slide, "previous" undoes a step. It reports its state to whoever follows it (`state()`: the page, the number of pages, the slide, the next and previous ones - hidden ones skipped -, the click animations and how many are left; `onState` on every change) and takes the same gestures from elsewhere (`next`, `prev`, `jump`): that is how the presenter view drives it. For the one window the presenter view drives (`options.screens`) it also has the black screen (B) and the white screen (W) - a veil that covers everything (`setScreen`: the same key removes it, a click on it and any navigation give the slide back, the timed advance waits behind it and counts again on return: `armAdvance`) - and the pen's drawing (`inkLayer`: an SVG layer inside the page's frame, which follows its scale; it reads only numbers, at most 256 per send, brought back inside the page, and keeps at most 400 strokes; the stroke is smoothed, not the polyline of the mouse positions: `smoothInk` brings each point back to the average of its neighbors along the stroke (a Gaussian kernel of 1.2 % of the page's longer side), the two ends staying still, then `curveOf` turns them into Bézier arcs through the middle of each segment - the same calculation in the console and in the window, on points kept at least 2 page pixels apart (`INK_STEP`: a point closer to the last one is only the stroke's tip, which bounds the work for a very fine stylus); it is erased when the page changes, and an effect's copy carries the drawing of the page left), driven by `screen`, `ink` and `clearInk`. `surface` (a page's frame, view and stage, enlarged to their box), `paintSteps` and `inkLayer` are shared by the player and the console. Nothing is redone at each step: the box size is kept by a `ResizeObserver` (`fit` never reads the layout) and transitions play between the page arrived at and ONE copy of the stage left, made at the start when a slide has an effect (otherwise at the first transition), kept off screen and brought up to date at each step (`buildSpare`, `takeGhost`, `parkSpare`); it goes behind or in front through the `pp-slide-above` class (the `z-index` of `slide-show.css`, inside the stage's stacking context: the bar and the note stay in front), never by a DOM move. |

App side: `open` opens `present.html` in a named window (▶ replaces it instead of stacking another), in the gesture; the page tells its opener it is ready, the app sends it its texts then the measured rendering (`pack`: the style sheets, the rendering's slot, the `--pp-` variables, the page cut, what the player reads from the editor); it listens only to the window it opened (`event.source`, the page's origin or "null" in GFS's sandbox), reads only three messages - "ready", "state" (re-read by `readState`: whole numbers and booleans, nothing else) and "goodbye" - and releases the rendering when the window closes; `follow` follows the window (the state - including the black or white screen, re-read as 0, 1 or 2 -, a watcher that sees the browser close it, and `onReady`, every time the page says it is ready: reloaded, it starts again from zero), `command` sends it an order (next, previous, go to, screen, stroke, erase: the `COMMANDS` list; the pen's points, a list of numbers, go only in `ink`), `cancel` closes it, `duplicate` copies the measured rendering (the preview of the next slide). Page side: `unpack` rebuilds that rendering identically (fonts and sheets loaded before the first display) and `clean` removes from the received HTML whatever would run (script, frame, object, form, base, meta, link, `on…` handlers, `javascript:` addresses). Only this file and `slide-present.js` talk to a window other than GFS's parent frame. |
| `extensions/diapositives/slide-present.js` and `present.html` | The separate window's page. `present.html` (content security policy: no inline script nor `eval`, no frame, object, base or form) loads the app's `dom.js`, `color-math.js`, `slide-props.js`, `frame-anim.js`, `frame-link.js` and `slide-transitions.js`, then `slide-player.js`, `slide-window.js` and `slide-present.js`, which waits for the texts and then the rendering from its opener (and only from it), rebuilds it and has it played. The first click or key (not Esc, not a lone modifier, not a shortcut) only asks for full screen, the one gesture the browser grants in a page that has just opened; Esc, "Exit", the end followed by a gesture, or leaving full screen close the window; opened alone, the page says where it opens from. It tells the app where the slide show is ("state": numbers and booleans) and when it ends ("goodbye", once; a window closed by the browser, or reloaded, does not say it: the app sees it itself), and obeys its orders (next, previous, go to: the same gesture as the page's keyboard; black or white screen and the pen's stroke, which the player has only if the app said `speaker`); the presenter view uses this. These two files are served alone: the widget never loads them (`standalone` in the `js/extensions.js` registry). |
| `extensions/diapositives/slide-speaker.js` (and `slide-speaker.css`) | The presenter view (`SlideSpeaker`): the console that takes the panel over while the slide show plays in its window. Four tools at the right edge of the current slide's title: the audience's black screen (B) and white screen (W) (pressed according to the state the window reported; the help sentence turns negative while the audience sees a screen), the pen (D) - it draws with the mouse on the copy of the current slide (`mirror`, the same layer as the window's) and sends the points in batches (every 30 ms at most, 64 points at most: a click then does not move to the next slide, Esc ends it before quitting; nothing is drawn on the end screen, behind a screen, or outside the page) - and erasing (E; changing page erases too). Each tool shows, on hover (after 0.35 s) or keyboard focus, a bubble (its name, then what it does: `toolTip`, `showTip`); it has no `title` but a detail that `aria-describedby` reads. The slide show's keys, the wheel over the previews, Tab that never leaves the console (a modal window). It talks to no window: `slide-show.js` links it to the slide show's (`SlideWindow`) through `setState`, `reset` and `onCommand`, and the notes, which come from the template, go in through `textContent`. No library. At the level of the floating bars, under the menus; colors: the tokens of `css/style.css`. |
| `extensions/diapositives/slide-transitions.js`, `slide-transitions-ui.js` (and `slide-transitions.css`) | A slide's transitions: the effects (`SlideTransitions`: fade, push, wipe, cover, uncover, zoom, then the shape effects - split, blinds, circle, diamond, plus - which reveal the arriving page through a `clip-path` (a polygon with the same number of vertices, `frames` for plus), described for going forward by `forward`, played backwards when going back by `plan`, as the browser's animations - `translate`, `scale`, `opacity`, `clip-path` - between two layers it is given by `play`; the system's reduced motion turns any effect into a 0.3 s fade); the "Transition…" window of a thumbnail's ⋯ menu (`SlideTransitionsUi`: a draft in three groups of radio buttons, a preview that replays the effect at each choice, "Apply", "Apply to all", `describe` for the badge's tooltip). The slide show's player (`slide-player.js`: `go`, `takeGhost`, `beginTransition`, `endTransition`) plays them between the page left, a copy of the stage made once and kept off screen, and the page arrived at, stacked by the `pp-slide-above` class (`stack`); the strip (`slide-strip.js`: the menu row, `syncTransitions`) shows the badge. No library. |
| `extensions/diapositives/slide-background-ui.js` (and `slide-background.css`) | The "Background…" window of a thumbnail's ⋯ menu (`SlideBackgroundUi`: a draft - the palette of every color menu (`ColorPalette`), the template's and the document's saved colors (`ColorStore`), "No background", "Custom…" which opens `ColorDialog` on top -, a preview of the page and the color's code, a warning under the preview when the editor's text would not be readable on it (`readsOn`: under 4.5:1; a `status` region, empty when the background is readable), "Apply" (`SlideProps.applyBackground`, a single history step), "Apply to all", the palette's keyboard: Tab enters on the chosen color, the arrows, Home and End). The strip (`slide-strip.js`: the menu row) opens it; the background's drawing is in `js/slide-props.js` and `css/free-frame.css`. No library. |
| `extensions/diapositives/slide-themes.js`, `slide-theme-ui.js` (and `slide-theme-ui.css`) | The theme of a presentation, extension side: `slide-themes.js` (`SlideThemes`: the seven ready-made themes - data only, fonts among the six of the menu, text and titles at 4.5:1 or better on the background -, `find`, `byId`, `nameOf`, and the DOCUMENT's theme list: `saved`, `save`, `remove`, `freeName`, `isReadOnly`, `wire` - twelve at most, read back by `cleanSaved` from JSON another version may have written, in the `themes` key of the document's reserved settings row (`Templates.updateDocumentSettings`), one Ctrl + Z step per gesture through `SettingsHistory.track`) and `slide-theme-ui.js` (`SlideThemeUi`: the "Theme…" window opened by the Page menu row, `SlidesEntry.openTheme`; a draft - the "Original look" list, the document's themes and then the ready-made themes as a radio group (`Dom.radioGroup`); at the bottom, left of "Cancel", "Save as theme…" (`saveDraft`: `Dialogs.prompt`, `SlideThemes.save`) and "Remove from list" (`forgetChosen`), greyed with their reason (`aria-disabled`); `SlideThemes.wire` is called when the window is created (`ensure`), never when the script runs: an extension's scripts load in parallel, a slide preview in the draft's colors and fonts, the two fonts, nine swatches that open `ColorDialog` without "keep" boxes (`keep: false`), the readability warning under the swatches -, the "Decoration…" button under the two fonts (`openDecor` opens `SlideDecorUi`; `setDecor` keeps the theme's name: "Ocean" with a logo stays "Ocean"; a ready-made theme keeps the decor you set, "Original look" removes it, `covers` recognizes a document theme by its colors, fonts and decor), "Apply" (`commit`: `HistoryMarkers.begin('page')`, `PageLayout.setTheme`, `SlideTheme.remap`, a single Ctrl + Z step), "Cancel" and Esc which change nothing). |
| `extensions/diapositives/slide-decor-ui.js` (and `slide-decor-ui.css`) | The theme's "Decoration…" window (`SlideDecorUi`, opened by the "Theme…" window's button: `open({ theme, onApply })`): a decor draft (`workOf` / `decorOf`, bounded by `SlideTheme.normalizeDecor`), a slide preview (`paintDecor`: `SlideTheme.decorItems`' decor scaled to the paper), four rows - the band (edge, thickness, eight `Dom.radioGroup` swatches), the logo (`GridXlsxImport.chooseFile`, `ImageIo.load`, then `logoFrom`, which redraws the image smaller - `SIDES`, never enlarged - as PNG then, without transparency, as JPEG until `SlideTheme.LOGO_SRC_MAX`; an SVG is rasterized; 10 MB at most; `fileTooBig`, `unreadable`, `tooBig` change nothing), the number, the footer text - and the "not on the first one" box; fields with no purpose are greyed (`render`), "Remove all" empties the draft, "Cancel" and Esc change nothing, "Apply" (or Enter in the footer text) hands the decor back to `SlideThemeUi` (`commit`). |
| `extensions/diapositives/slide-notes-ui.js` (and `slide-notes.css`) | The "Notes…" window of a thumbnail's ⋯ menu (`SlideNotesUi`: a draft - an input field of `NOTES_MAX` characters at most (10,000) with its label, its help sentence and its counter -, "Apply" or Ctrl+Enter (`SlideProps.applyNotes`, a single history step; emptied notes, or notes made of blanks, remove the notes), "Cancel" or Esc which write nothing; for one slide at a time). The strip (`slide-strip.js`: the menu row and the badge) opens it; the notes live in the marker of `js/slide-props.js` and go out nowhere. No library. |

### Orchestration

`js/main.js` (3,800 lines): templates, Edit and Reading modes, autosave, exports (one file, one batch),
wiring of the gallery and of Grist. It is the file that knows all the others.

### Stylesheets

`css/style.css` (color tokens, light and dark theme, `--font-ui`), `css/toolbar-v2.css` (the top bar),
`css/editor-v2.css` (the editor's text); the other 53 each go with a module or a window of `js/`
(`css/callout.css` for `js/callout.js`, `css/gfs-ambiance.css` for `js/gfs-ambiance.js`) and say so in their first comment; `css/roboto-fonts.css` embeds
the documents' font.

## Where the widget touches the outside

- **Grist**: `js/grist-api.js` for the most part; the modules that own a table read and write it
  themselves (`js/templates.js`, `js/template-preferences.js`, `js/comments.js`, `js/text-expansion.js`,
  `js/saved-page-formats.js`, `js/versions-store.js`, and `extensions/edition/live-channel.js` for the live-editing table). Nine `Publipostage_*` tables, created only with the person's consent
  (`js/table-consent.js`); the widget neither deletes nor renames any table or column. In the tables that
  hold the data, a single write: the date of the last PDF export, in the column the person chose
  (`js/export-date.js`, called by `js/main.js` after an export).
- **Grist Factory Suite**: only when the widget's address carries `gfs=` (GFS hosts it), `js/gfs-link.js` writes to
  the parent frame (the context, `installer`, `stockage.ecrire`, `barre.masquer`, the answer to the `ouvrir` call, and `ranger`, a PDF the app has just made, only when the person chose “Save to GFS”) and reads what GFS sends it (the
  `extensions`, `langue`, `barre` and `ambiance` events, the `ouvrir` call), after checking where it comes from; alone, the widget writes and listens to
  no message.
- **The network**: the Grist API script (`docs.getgrist.com`), the editor (`esm.sh`), the export libraries and
  the Markdown import reader on first use (`cdnjs.cloudflare.com`, `cdn.jsdelivr.net`), the widget's own export engines and an
  extension's files (same site, `extensions/`), and an extension's library at the address GFS gives (Plotly for the page chart and ECharts for the created chart, with their
  integrity hash; the assembled live-editing module and the PDF-reading one, by `<script type="module">` in `Extensions.loadModuleLibrary`), all the others loaded by `ExportCommon.loadScriptOnce`, the two ambiance modules of GFS's kit (same site, `kit/js/`, by `import()` and the import map of `index.html`, only when GFS hosts the widget), the gallery catalog (same site, `fetch` in `js/template-gallery.js`), and the reading of an image (`fetch` or
  `new Image()`, in `js/image-io.js` only: insertion and the PDF, Word and Excel exports take it from
  there; an image from another site waits for a "Show" click on screen, and for the window of
  `js/external-images.js` on export). These
  are reads: the code has no `fetch` with a body or a write method, no `XMLHttpRequest`, no `WebSocket`, no
  `sendBeacon` and no cookie.
- **The browser**: `localStorage` for the language, the theme, the formatting of bubbles and chips in the editor, the
  trigger keys, the shortcuts and the view of their panel, the mail program of the Email mode, autosave, the last choice in
  the pre-print assembly, the destination of exported PDFs, the settings of the protected PDF (permissions, author, the
  way its password is given, the password column: never a password), the last version whose news window was closed, the
  zoom level of the last templates opened (with their number and name), the short list of the templates whose images in front of or behind the text have already had their position checked (signatures) and the snapshot of table and column names
  (`js/schema-renames.js`). Never a template's content or a piece of the document's data. All of it, except the language
  (`js/i18n.js`) and the names snapshot, goes through `js/person-store.js`: hosted by GFS, whose isolated frame refuses the
  browser's storage, these preferences go to the space GFS keeps for the app.
- **Downloads**: `ExportCommon.downloadBlob`, on the person's click. A PDF can also, if the person chooses, be saved to GFS instead of downloaded, or as well (`js/pdf-delivery.js`).

## What is not widget code

`templates-gallery/` (the gallery's templates, data with their `catalogue.json`), `img/` (the logo),
`screenshots/` (the README's screenshots) and the documents at the root: `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`,
`SECURITY.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `PATCH_NOTES.md`, `NOTICE`, `LICENSE` and this file. The page
loads none of them. `kit/` is Grist Factory's kit, taken as it is with its licence (style sheets and modules that its
apps share): the widget imports only `ambiance-message.js` and `ambiance-pose.js` (which imports `once.js`), and only when
GFS hosts it. `beta/` is the 1.0.0-beta.1 beta as it was published, kept at its address (see the [README](README.md)):
this map describes the current version, not that one. The project's development repository also keeps its tests, its
publication tools and its design notes, which are not published here.

## Where to change what

| To… | See |
|---|---|
| Add a column to the templates table | `js/templates.js` (creation, `ensureColumns`, reading, saving); the column must exist before the first write, otherwise Grist refuses the whole batch. `Jeton` is the exception: created on demand by `ensureToken`, never written by saving; so is `ModifiePar`, written only when `modifiedByColumnReady` says so (without structure rights, a write to a missing column would cancel the whole batch). |
| Add a window | `js/modal-base.js` and `js/dialogs.js`, `css/modal-base.css`. |
| Add a choice of column, table or template | `js/search-select.js`. |
| Change what a bookmark is, where it goes, or what the links that lead to it do | `js/anchors.js` (the attribute, the normalisation, the targets), `js/bookmark-dialog.js` (the window) and the "In this document" choice of `js/link-dialog.js`; each export writes the bookmark and the link its own way (`placeAnchor` in `js/pdf-export.js`, `anchorBookmarksOf` in `js/docx-export.js`, `anchorTarget` in `js/html-export.js`, `anchorMark` in `js/markdown-export.js`, `anchors` in `js/markdown-import.js`) and Reading mode follows them (`followInternalLink` in `js/reader-mode.js`). |
| Add a text to the interface | `js/i18n.js`, in French and English in the same batch. |
| Change how the widget follows GFS's language | `js/gfs-link.js` (`language`, `onLanguage`), `gfsLang` of `js/i18n.js` (the starting language), `applyLanguageLock` and `followGfsLanguage` of `js/settings.js` (the greyed boxes, the reload), `Settings.followGfsLanguage()` at the end of `init()` in `js/main.js`, the `#settings-language-gfs-note` sentence of `index.html`. |
| Change what the editor takes from GFS's ambiance (colours, rounded corners, shadows, fonts, backdrop) | `js/gfs-ambiance.js` (the posing and the two marks on `<html>`), `css/gfs-ambiance.css` (what a fallback cannot say), the stylesheets of `css/` (each reads `var(--surface)`, `var(--radius-md, 8px)`… with its original value as fallback) and `dev-tests/ambiance-exceptions.json` (why a hard-coded colour stays); `js/gfs-link.js` (`ambiance`, `onAmbiance`) only reads the message. A new colour or radius is read through a token, never hard-coded: section 31 of `dev-tests/verify-code-hygiene.mjs` refuses the rest. |
| Add a preference of the person (a setting to find again on the next page, in GFS too) | `js/person-store.js` (`PersonStore.get`, `set`, `remove`): never `localStorage` directly; the `pp_…` key is named in the README's paragraph about storage, in French and in English; reading never writes, and a module that reads its preference at load time subscribes to `PersonStore.onLoaded` to take it again when GFS's space arrives. |
| Add a menu or a floating window | `js/layers.js` (the stacking order). |
| Say why a button or a menu row is greyed | `js/lock-reasons.js` (the cause and its sentence; the tooltip of a greyed control comes from here, not from a `title =` set by hand), the `lock.*` keys of `js/i18n.js`, the `[data-pp-lock-blocks]` rules of `css/toolbar-v2.css`. |
| Add a keyboard shortcut | `js/shortcuts.js`. |
| Change what the "Protected PDF" protects (password, permissions, properties) | `js/pdf-protection.js` (the settings, the password of each row and pdfmake's fields), `js/pdf-protection-dialog.js` and `css/pdf-protection.css` (the window), `js/templates.js` (the shared password: `MotDePassePdf` column), `js/pdf-export.js` (`withDocFields`: the fields are only set on the finished document), `js/main.js` (`SINGLE_EXPORTS.pdfProtected`, `BATCH_EXPORTS.pdfProtectedZip`, `protectionForRecord`), `js/batch-failures.js` (the row without a password). A file combining several PDFs (a single PDF, sheet assembly) cannot be encrypted: those outputs are greyed out for this type. |
| Add a PDF export path | `js/main.js`: hand the PDFs to `PdfDelivery.deliver` (`js/pdf-delivery.js`) with the way to download them, so that they can also be saved to GFS according to the chosen destination; then call `stampExportDate` (the Settings > View column that keeps the last export date), with only the rows whose PDF has gone somewhere (the report's `delivered`: a PDF the person refused to save went nowhere). |
| Change where an exported PDF goes (downloaded, saved to GFS, both) | `js/pdf-delivery.js` (`plan`, `deliver`, `describe`, the `SETTING` that can be undone), the three `data-destination` rows of `index.html` (`syncExportMenus` and `wireExportMenus` of `js/main.js` show them for the two PDF types only and grey the ones that save without GFS), `GfsLink.deposit` of `js/gfs-link.js` (the `ranger` call), `exportCurrentRecord` of `js/pdf-export.js` (a single row's PDF) and `finishBatchExport` of `js/main.js` (the archive, the merged PDF, the sheet assembly). The sentences: `shelve.*` and `destination.*` of `js/i18n.js`. |
| Change what makes a PDF page a "blank page", or the windows that report it | `js/pdf-export.js` (`blankPagesOf` and `paintsInBody`: what is painted in the body; `markFurniture`: the header, footer, watermark and background that do not count; `blankPageBlock`: the page asked for between two page breaks; `renderOnce`: the layout done a single time, with no blank-page search for a presentation, whose every page is an intended slide), `js/batch-failures.js` (`confirmBlankPages` before saving, `showBlankPages` at the end of a batch) and their `blankPages.*` texts in `js/i18n.js`. A new PDF export path that saves a file goes through `exportCurrentRecord` or reads `blankPages` from `getNativePdfBlobForRecord`. |
| Add an export library | `js/export-common.js` (loader, integrity hash) and the security policy of `index.html`. |
| Change what the PowerPoint import recognizes, or what the PowerPoint export writes | `js/pptx-import.js` and `js/pptx-export.js` go together: what one writes (the place of a line, a transition's duration, highlighting), the other must read back. A slide text's page number is written as a slide-number field in the style of the element that holds its pill, not in the pill's own style (`runsOf` of `js/pptx-export.js`). |
| Change what the Markdown import recognizes, or what the Markdown export writes | `js/markdown-import.js` and `js/markdown-export.js` go together: what one writes, the other must read back. |
| Change what the HTML export writes, keeps or leaves out | `js/html-export.js`: the allow-lists (`KEPT_TAGS`, `STYLE_PROPS`, the attributes), the stylesheet blocks (`stylesheetFor`: one per family of elements, written only when the document uses it) and what sets the complete file apart from the content to paste (`BASE_CSS` is only written in the first). |
| Add a type to the "Export type" menu | A `data-export-type` row of the menu (and its `<option>` of `#v2-export-type`) in `index.html`, `SINGLE_EXPORTS` and `BATCH_EXPORTS` of `js/main.js`, its texts in `js/i18n.js`. The menu, with the three PDF qualities, must stay whole in a 700 × 400 panel: at eight types it is already tightened (`css/editor-v2.css`, `#v2-quality-flyout`). |
| Change what browser print prints (sheets, a page taller than a sheet, the page size) | `js/print-export.js` and `css/print.css`; the rendering itself is the Reading view's (`ReaderMode.renderInto` in `js/reader-mode.js`): changing it changes the print too. One way to print (on its own or hosted): a content rule is written once in `css/print.css`, under `div.pp-print-scope`; what the rendering must inherit from the page (the language, the font of `body`, the light tokens) goes through `lang` on `.pp-print-scope`, `font-family: inherit` and `:root, .pp-print-host` in `css/style.css`, because the container is `all: initial`. |
| Make a component work with GFS's bar (keep it out of its zone, follow it when it changes edge, hide it) | `js/gfs-bar.js` (`GfsBar.edge`, `zone`, `overlaps`, `limits`, `apart`, `bite`, `settle`, `keepClear`, `onChange`, `hide`) or, in CSS, `<html data-gfs-bar>` and the `--gfs-bar-*` variables: never a hard-coded number, the zone comes from GFS; only `js/gfs-link.js` and `js/gfs-bar.js` touch the bar itself. Already done: `js/toolbar-gap.js` (the toolbar's three rows, at the four edges), windows (`css/modal-base.css`, CSS only), the toolbar's menus (`positionFlyout`, `js/editor-core.js`) and tooltips (`fitTooltip`, `js/viewport-fit.js`), the popups, choice lists, bubbles and floating panels (`ViewportFit.avoidBar`, which calls `GfsBar.settle`: `placePopup`, `createFloatingPanel`, `SearchSelect`, `TemplateTreeSelect`, a link's tooltip and the size bubble of a column or row drag), the slide strip and the "Import" panel (a gutter, `GfsBar.bite`), the zoom pill and the ▶ (`js/page-zoom.js:stepAside`), the slide show (`GfsBar.hide`), the logo (`js/toolbar-gap.js`) and the Reading mode exit (`CleanReading.wire`), through `GfsBar.keepClear`. |
| Open in a new tab a link that GFS's isolated frame does not let be followed (`mailto:`, `tel:`) | `GfsLink.opensInNewTab(href)` (`js/gfs-link.js`), called wherever the widget makes or renders a link: `onCreateEmail` (`js/main.js`), `openExternal` (`js/link-dialog.js`), `readerWrapperFrom` (`js/reader-mode.js`). |
| Add an export engine of the widget (a script that only an export uses) | An inert `<script type="text/x-lazy-engine" data-engine="…">` tag in `index.html`, then its name in the `engines` list of the export that uses it (`js/main.js`): `js/export-engines.js` loads it when needed. |
| A new document element (node, formatting) | `js/editor-nodes.js` (the editor), its sheet in `css/` (screen and Reading mode), then `js/pdf-export.js` and `js/docx-export.js` (and `js/pptx-export.js`, if the element can go into a presentation): each export converts it separately. |
| Change the color palette, or add a color menu | `js/color-palette.js` (the palette and its rows; a menu plugs in through `createMenu`), `js/color-dialog.js` (the "Custom color" window), `js/color-store.js` (the kept colors: `PageLayout.getCustomColors` for the template, `Templates.getDocumentSettings` for the document), `css/color-palette.css`. |
| Put variable bubbles or chips (date, time, user's email and name) in a text field (Subject, To, Cc, Bcc, PDF name) | `js/field-editor.js` (a one-line editor that behaves like the `<input>` it replaces), `js/field-codec.js` (the saved value: plain text or HTML), `ReaderMode.fieldText` and `ReaderMode.smartChipValue` in `js/reader-mode.js` (the resolved value, and the chips' value, including the time style of a "Current time" chip), `FloatingToolbars.createTimeChipToolbar` in `js/floating-toolbars.js` (that chip's bar, in the document as in a field), `css/field-editor.css`. |
| Change what the "Otherwise show" of a conditional value, a conditional block or a variable with a condition writes (text and variables) | `buildOtherwiseTextFields` and `mountTextFields` in `js/variable-condition.js` (the field in the window, `FieldEditor.attach` with `hostWindow`), `VariableOtherwise.fieldOf` and `storedOf` in `js/variable-otherwise.js` (what the field shows of a bubble and what it saves: the old object as long as it says the same thing), `FieldCodec.linesOf`, `toStored` (option `keepSpaces`) and `toStoredLines` in `js/field-codec.js` (what is saved), `ConditionalText.otherwiseItems` and `resolve` in `js/conditional-text.js`, `ConditionalValue.resolve` in `js/conditional-value.js` and `VariableOtherwise.resolve` in `js/variable-otherwise.js` (what Read mode and the exports write), `otherwiseTablesOf` and `rewriteElement` in `js/schema-renames.js` (renames in Grist), `bubbleParts` and `replaceInBubble` in `js/find-replace.js` (Find / Replace), `otherwiseCites` in `js/variables.js` (the confirmation before deleting a link rule). |
| Change what the "If no row" text of a loop writes (text and variables) | `mountEmptyField` and `emptyShown` in `js/variable-loop.js` (the field in the window, `FieldEditor.attach` with `hostWindow: 'loop'`, and the preview sentence), `emptyNodes` in `js/loop-rules.js` (what the zone writes in place of the rows: the turn's row of the zone around the loop gives its own to the variables), `resolveEmptyLoopText` in `js/reader-mode.js` (the bubbles of an in-sentence loop, in a text field too), `rewriteLoop` and `explicitTablesOf` in `js/schema-renames.js` (renames in Grist), `loopCites` in `js/variables.js` (the confirmation before deleting a link rule), `loopWindowTaken` in `js/floating-toolbars.js` (the toolbar of a variable of the field greys out Loop). |
| Change what becomes of the spaces around a value that writes nothing (a removed conditional value, an empty or hidden bubble) | `js/space-gap.js` (the rule: `absorb` and `settle`); it is called by `putValue` in `js/reader-mode.js` (the four places where a bubble is replaced by its value) and by the removal of a value in `js/conditional-value.js`: a new place that puts a value in a bubble's stead calls it too. |
| Change what an e-mail template accepts (a key, a paste, formatting left over from an older template) or the lines of the link's text | `js/email-plain-text.js` and `css/email-plain-text.css` for what the editor accepts; `js/mailto-export.js` for the link's text, whose lines are the editor's lines, one for one. |
| Make the link readable by a mail program that misreads it (Zimbra shows `&amp;` for "&" and turns "+" into a space) | `js/mailto-export.js` (`forZimbra`, the `client` option of `buildMailtoUrl`, `getMailClient` and `setMailClient`) and the Email tab of `js/settings.js` and `index.html`. |
| Change what the bubbles and chips of the editor show of their formatting (Settings > Theme > Bubbles and chips) | `setBubbleMarks`, `applyBubbleMarks` and the `pp_bubble_marks` key of `js/settings.js`; `showTextMarks` of `js/editor-nodes.js` and `chipView` of `js/mention.js`, which read the marks in the view of each kind, page number, footnote and conditional box included (never in the saved HTML); the `:where(:root[data-bubble-marks])` rules of `css/variable-actions.css`, loaded before `css/find-replace.css`; the two `settings-bubble-marks` boxes of `index.html`. |
| Change what Reading, the PDF, the Word file and the HTML write of the color, underline and strikethrough set on a footnote, a conditional box or the chip of a Yes / No variable set to a box | `textMarksOf`, `carryBoxLines` and `carryNoteColors` of `js/reader-mode.js` (the marks read on the elements that wrap the chip; a footnote's color goes into `--pp-note-color`, which the `.reader-content .footnote-ref-marker` rule of `css/editor-v2.css` reads; `carryBoxLines` puts the lines set on a Yes / No variable's chip on each box of its value), `resolve` of `js/conditional-checkbox.js` (the box's underline and strikethrough, as `data-checkbox-lines` on the drawn box: Reading draws them with the `.resolved-checkbox[data-checkbox-lines…]` rules of `css/editor-v2.css`), `checkboxRuns` of `js/pdf-export.js` (`decoration`) and `checkboxRuns` and `footnoteRuns` of `js/docx-export.js` (the box's strikethrough; the reference's color, lines and highlight), `checkboxSpan` and `footnoteRefInto` of `js/html-export.js` (the box's `pp-struck` and `pp-underlined` classes and the color on the footnote's link, which `CHECKBOX_CSS` draws). These outputs do not read the Settings choice. |
| Change the margin presets or the chain that links the four margins (Settings > Margins) | `MARGIN_PRESETS`, `wireMarginPresets`, `wireMarginLink` and `applyMargins` of `js/settings.js` (the preset in place is the one `boundedMarginsMm` of `js/page-layout.js` would set identically), the five buttons of `index.html` (`settings-margin-preset-*`, `settings-margins-link`), the `marginsLinked` and `marginsUnlinked` icons of `js/icons.js`, `.settings-margins-presets` and `.settings-margins-grid` of `css/toolbar-v2.css`, `.settings-margins-link` of `css/variable-actions.css` (the same button as "Compare with another column"), `READ_ONLY_DISABLED_INPUTS` of `js/main.js` (greyed out when read-only). |
| Change the color and size of a list item's bullet, dash or number | `js/list-markers.js` (what counts: the start of the item's text, its color and size; `createExtension` for the editor, `apply` for Reading, `styleOfElement` for the exports), the two `li::marker` rules of `css/editor-v2.css` (the reset on the `<li>` stops a sublist from receiving its parent's setting), `markerLookOf` and `listMarkerRun` of `js/pdf-export.js` (the bullet written in the text's color and size, its hanging indent at its width; `getComputedStyle(item, '::marker')` for a rotated frame), `markerRunOf` of `js/docx-export.js` (the paragraph mark), the bullet's size in `collectItem` of `js/pptx-export.js` (no `buSzPct` when it equals the text's; `a:buClr` for an own color, `markerColor`) and `listItemInto` of `js/html-export.js`. |
| Change the style of a heading level (what "Update Heading N" keeps, how headings look) | `js/heading-styles.js` (`captureStyle` for what is kept, `declarations` and `apply` for what is written, `createExtension` for the editor), the `levelStyles` attribute of `headingNumberingConfig` in `js/editor-nodes.js`, the two blocks of the Heading menu (their rows `#v2-btn-heading-style-update`, `#v2-btn-heading-style-reset`, `#v2-btn-heading-style-doc-update` and `#v2-btn-heading-style-doc-reset`, the headings `#v2-heading-scope-*` and the previews `#v2-heading-levels-*` in `index.html`, `css/heading-styles.css`, `wireHeadingMenu` and `syncToolbarState` of `js/main-toolbar.js`), the document's style (`documentStyles`, `effective` and `updateShared` of `js/heading-styles.js`, `getDocumentSetting` and the `pp:documentSettingsChanged` notice of `js/templates.js`), the `apply` calls of `resolveReaderBody` and `preview` in `js/reader-mode.js`, `headingMarkerRun` of `js/pdf-export.js` and the marker of `paragraphBlockFrom` in `js/docx-export.js` (a heading's number follows its look); the `headingStyle.*` keys of `js/i18n.js`. |
| Change the format painter's saved formats (the number of places, what is kept, where and how they live, the menu) | `js/format-slots.js` (`MAX` comes from `PageLayout.CUSTOM_FORMATS_MAX`, `slotOf` and `snapshotOf` for what is kept and repainted, `SCOPES` for the two scopes), `CustomFormats` in `js/page-layout.js` (`normalizeCustomFormats`: the bounds of the template's JSON), `styleOfMarks` and `marksOf` in `js/typing-style.js`, `paint` and `isUsable` in `js/format-painter.js`, `js/format-slots-menu.js` and the `#v2-painter-group` group of `index.html` (the menu, its rows), `css/format-painter.css`, `FormatSlots.wire` in `js/main.js`, `FormatSlotsMenu.wire` and `sync` in `js/main-toolbar.js`, the `fmtSlot.*` keys of `js/i18n.js`. |
| Change the theme of a presentation (what it sets, where it shows, what follows when it changes) | `js/slide-theme.js` (`normalize` for the bounds, `refresh` and `defaultsAt` for the screen, `forOutput` and `paperOf` for the outputs, `remap` for the recoloring), `css/slide-theme.css`, `withPaper` of `js/slide-props.js`, the `theme` key of `js/page-layout.js` (`setMarginsMm`, `getTheme`, `setTheme`, `snapshot`, `restore`, `getMarginsPt`), `outputHtml` of `js/slides-entry.js` and its callers (`js/main.js`, `js/macro-templates.js`, `extensions/diapositives/slide-show.js`), `themeXml` and `runStyleOf` of `js/pptx-export.js`, the `themeRow` row of `js/color-store.js` and its drawing in `js/color-palette.js`, `shapeLook` / `lineLook` of `js/free-frame.js`, the `#v2-btn-slide-theme` row of the Page menu (`js/orientation-toggle.js`), the font chip and color icon of `syncTextStyleChips` (`js/main-toolbar.js`), the files of `extensions/diapositives/` above (including the document's theme list: `SlideThemes.save` / `remove` / `saved`); the decor: `normalizeDecor`, `decorItems`, `decorElement`, `createExtension` and `decorHtmlFor` of `js/slide-theme.js`, `forOutput` (`decor` option) of `js/slide-props.js`, `decorHtml` of `js/free-frame.js`, `pageSizeOf` and `outputHtml` (`stored` option) of `js/slides-entry.js`, `decorPartsOf`, `masterXml` and `firstLayoutXml` of `js/pptx-export.js`, `extensions/diapositives/slide-decor-ui.js` and the thumbnails' decor in `slide-strip.js`; the `slides.theme.*`, `slides.decor.*` and `color.scope.theme*` keys of `js/i18n.js`. |
| Change the line spacing, the space before and after or the indents of a paragraph (the "Paragraph…" window) | `js/paragraph-format.js` (the bounds and the writing: `markup`; the window: `open`; `readOf` for whoever reads it; `indentableAt` to know whether the indent counts there), the `#v2-btn-paragraph` row of `index.html` and its wiring in `syncBlockButtons` and `wireToolbar` of `js/main-toolbar.js`, `ParagraphFormat.createExtension` in `structureExtensions` of `js/editor.js`, the `paragraphFormat.*` keys of `js/i18n.js`, Reading (the inline style, nothing to write), `applyParagraphFormat`, `addLeftMargin` and `retuneLineHeight` of `js/pdf-export.js` (the line spacing divided by Roboto's native one, the margins that add up, the left indent as a margin and the first-line indent as `leadingIndent`), `spacingOf` and `indentOf` of `js/docx-export.js` (`w:spacing`: before on the first piece, after on the last; `w:ind`: `left`, then `firstLine` or `hanging` on the first piece only), `paragraphPropertiesXml` of `js/pptx-export.js` (`lnSpc` as a percentage of the single line, `spcBef` and `spcAft` from the measured padding, `marL` and `indent` from the measured indent), `SPACE_PROPS` and `INDENT_PROPS` of `js/html-export.js` (the padding of a `<p>`; the indent of a `<p>` in a list or a quote is not written), the `spacing` snapshot of `js/format-painter.js` (the indents never put into a list or a quote), and the reverse way: `spacingOf` and `paragraphTag` of `js/pptx-import.js` (`marL` and `indent`, at the page's scale). |
| Make a page gesture (size, orientation, margins, watermark, template colors) part of Ctrl + Z | `HistoryMarkers.track('page', …)` of `js/history-markers.js` around the gesture (or `begin` then `commit` when it stretches, like the digits of a typed margin in `js/settings.js`), `snapshot` and `restore` of `js/page-layout.js` (what the marker keeps and gives back), `domains` for one more state outside the text; `anywhere` on Undo and Redo in `js/shortcuts.js` (the key also acts when the focus is neither in the editor nor in a field where one types - it leaves a list, a slider, a checkbox or a radio button that the floating bar keeps after an adjustment: `typesNothing` -, except inside a component that undoes by itself and carries `data-own-undo`, like the panel of `js/find-replace.js`). |
| Make the writing of a header or a footer part of Ctrl + Z | `hfSession` of `js/header-footer-preview.js` (`HistoryMarkers.begin('headerFooter')` when the first zone is entered, `commit({ alone: true })` when `exitHeaderFooterMode` leaves), `snapshot` and `restore` of that module (what the marker keeps and gives back), the `headerFooter` domain of `domains` in `js/history-markers.js` (the zones' text, the pill's boxes and the page). |
| Make a macro template's eye or composition part of Ctrl + Z | `HistoryMarkers.track('macro', …)` around the eye in `toggleModelHidden` of `js/macro-editor.js`, `snapshot` and `restore` of that module (the most recent composition, written back into the row by the `queueComposition` queue, the one the eye uses), the `macro` domain of `domains` in `js/history-markers.js`; the composition saved by the window: `recordComposition` after the macro template is reloaded (`begin('macro', before)`, `keep` then `resume` for the earlier history); Undo and Redo are no longer greyed out in `syncLocks` of `js/main-toolbar.js`, and `editorOnScreenWithoutFocus` of `js/shortcuts.js` counts the macro template's summary. |
| Make a field outside the text (the email's Subject, To, Cc, Bcc, the PDF name) part of Ctrl + Z | `open` (the field's focus opens the session: `HistoryMarkers.begin('fields')`) and `settle` (leaving the field closes it: `commit({ alone: true })`, or the next `focusin`) in `js/field-editor.js`, `snapshot` and `restore` of that module (the stored value of each editable field, given back field by field with the `input` event for the autosave and `fieldrestored` for the PDF-name pencil and the "+ Bcc" button of `js/main.js`), the `fields` domain of `domains` in `js/history-markers.js`, `HistoryMarkers.flush()` before the editor's history in the Undo and Redo clicks of `js/main-toolbar.js`. |
| Make what the page shows of a document setting (the heading numbering) follow Ctrl + Z | `syncHeadingNumbering` in `js/main-toolbar.js`, called by `syncToolbarState` on every transaction: it re-reads the `headingNumberingConfig` node (`Editor.getHeadingNumberingStyle`) and copies its value onto the editor root (`data-heading-style`, which the headings' CSS and the summary view of `js/editor-nodes.js` read), onto the hidden `<select>` `#v2-heading-numbering-select` and onto the lit button of `#v2-numbering-seg`; it touches nothing while a header or footer is being written (`HeaderFooterPreview.getHfMode()`: the editor then holds only the zone). A document setting the page reads somewhere other than ProseMirror is re-read from the document this way, never only at the gesture. |
| Change when the editor's table of contents preview is redrawn | `createTocNode` in `js/editor-nodes.js`: `refresh` reads the headings from the page (`view.dom`), not from the document, so it only draws right on an up-to-date page. The NodeView is born DURING the view update, before ProseMirror has attached the nodes of the new content: its first drawing sees the previous headings or none. It is therefore redrawn on `update`, on the first `transaction` after its creation (`drawnOnUpdatedPage`: a load that does not emit `update`, like `loadTrackedDocument` at "Done" of a header or footer, left no other chance) and, afterwards, on every transaction that changed `data-heading-style`. Guarded by the `toc_editor_preview_*` cases of `pageBreakToc` and the `tocAfterDoneMouse` Node script. |
| Make "Accept all" and "Reject all" undo with a single Ctrl + Z | `runChunkedLibCommand` in `js/track-changes-commands.js`: the slices of `chunkSize` changes (200) advance on separate plugin-less states (`lib.EditorState.create`) and their steps are added to ONE transaction (`whole`) sent at the end, inside `withEndGuard`; `resolveInto`, the body that `resolveSuggestionIds` (the floating bar's "Accept" / "Reject") shares; `refuseAttributes(editor, whole)` (`onStall`) for the lone settings the library does not undo, and `releaseWidthsRefusedBy` (`adjust`) for the width of a column that became "automatic" again; the `acceptAllSuggestionsChunked` and `rejectAllSuggestionsChunked` commands of `js/track-changes.js` pick these two hooks. Do not attach the slices with the `appendedTransaction` meta (`js/page-copies.js`, `js/slide-copies.js` and `js/slide-links.js` would then let the transaction through without checking the linked-slide lock), nor with the `composition` meta (Tiptap's `clearDocument` plugin skips those transactions, and prosemirror-tables would "repair" a table between two slices). The single transaction carries the `bulkResolve` meta (`isBulkResolution`), read by the `coreExtensionOptions.delete.filterTransaction` filter of `init` in `js/editor.js`, and `preventAutolink`: without them Tiptap re-reads all of its steps, at a cost that grows with their square, for its `delete` event and for link creation (2.4 s instead of 0.7 s at 3,200 changes); `autolinkOf` replays the links plugin on each slice, to keep the links as before. |
| Make the messages of a comment thread part of Ctrl + Z | `recorded` (posting the first message with `publishes`, replying) and `deleteThread` in `js/comments.js`: `HistoryMarkers.begin('comments', undefined, commentId)` before the gesture, `commit` after (the marker joins the mark's step when the gesture opened one, or with `join: true` when the mark is still the last step: `composer.clean`); `snapshot` (the thread, message by message, with its `uid` that survives the recreated row) and `restore(next, prev)` (only the difference between the two states is written, by `queueWrites`, one after the other); the `comments` domain of `domains` in `js/history-markers.js`; `startComposer` and `discardNewThread` (a thread closed without a message gives back the history from before its mark); `syncPopup` (the open popup follows Undo); the `comments.updateFailed.*` keys of `js/i18n.js` (a refused write is told, the threads are re-read). |
| Make the template's name or an export choice part of Ctrl + Z | A state that belongs to the OPEN template is filed in its history, like the text, through a domain of `js/history-markers.js` (`read()` takes a copy, `write(next, prev)` gives back what the gesture changed): `HistoryMarkers.track('exportChoices', …)` in `choose` of `wireExportMenus` (`ExportChoices.snapshot` / `restore` in `js/export-choices.js`), and for the name `HistoryMarkers.define('name', …)` in `wireTemplateRename` of `js/main.js` with `begin` when the pencil opens and `commit({ alone: true })` when it is validated (nothing is filed if the name did not change: Escape); a state that ONLY `js/main.js` knows is declared with `define`. An action that reaches beyond the open template goes through `ActionHistory` instead (next row). |
| Make a library action (the ★, then the following ones) part of Ctrl + Z | `ActionHistory.add({ undo, redo })` AFTER the gesture succeeded (never before: a refused gesture leaves no marker), in the gesture's function in `js/main.js`: `defaultTemplateAction(from, to)` for the ★, `deletedTemplateAction(kept)` for deleting a template and `copiedTemplateAction(id, from, withTables)` for copying one ("Save as…": `onSaveAs`; the gallery: `saveAsNewTemplate`, called by `saveEntryAsTemplate` of `js/template-gallery-modal.js` - `undo` asks a question before removing the copy, `Dialogs.confirm`; `redo` gives it back through `Templates.restoreRow`) (`undo` gives back the state before, `redo` sets it again, each checks that the state is the one the action left - otherwise `ActionHistory.conflict(message)` - and returns the status-corner text; `kept` is the row read before the deletion by `Templates.snapshotRow`); `ActionHistory.wire({ status })` in `init()`; the `actions` domain of `js/history-markers.js`; the `history.*` keys of `js/i18n.js`; the line of the Undo tests' ledger (class `covered`) and its case in the `undoLibrary` group |
| Add a template to the gallery | A folder in `templates-gallery/` (the template's text, its `pack.json`: tables, columns, Linked tables rules, page and type, and its screenshots), then its line in `templates-gallery/catalogue.json` (name, tags, thumbnail, screenshots, "Features shown"). `js/template-pack.js` reads and checks the `pack.json`; tables that several templates share are described once, in the `families/` folder. |
| Change live editing (the pipe, the engine, the shared editor, the people bar, who saves) | `extensions/edition/`: `live-channel.js` (the `Publipostage_Direct` table), `live-sync.js` (Yjs, the rooms, compaction), `live-page.js` (the template's page: format, margins, theme, decor, shared and kept in each person's Undo), `live-editor.js` (the link with the editor, per-person Undo, the cursors, `peerPlaces`: where the others are), `live-control.js` (start, join, stop, the bar) and `live.css`; what the rest of the widget knows of it goes through `LiveEntry` (`js/live-entry.js`); the dots of the people under the thumbnails are in `extensions/diapositives/slide-strip.js` (`syncPeople`). The library is GFS's (`edition-live`): it is never imported. |
| Change a slide object's link (what it can aim at, the slide show actions, what the click does, the animation on click) | `js/frame-link.js` (the text and what it refuses, `frameAt`, `openUrl`, `canAnimate`, `transText`, `transOf`); `js/free-frame.js` (`link`, `linkTrans`, the toolbar's "Link…" button, the corner mark); `js/link-dialog.js` (`openForObject`, the "Slide show actions" group, the "Animation on click" field: its three lists, `syncTrans`); `js/slide-props.js` (`SIDE_CHOICES`, `choicesOf`, `DURATION_CHOICES`, the lists the "Transition…" window and the link's share); `extensions/diapositives/slide-player.js` (`followFrame`, `windowOfAnchor`); `js/reader-mode.js` (`followFrameLink`); `extensions/diapositives/present.html` (loads `js/frame-link.js`); PowerPoint: `js/pptx-export.js` (`objectLinkXml`, the object link written in `p:cNvPr`; an action is an `hlinkshowjump` with an empty `r:id`) and `js/pptx-import.js` (`frameLinkOf`, which makes a shape's or picture's link the frame's). Tests: `frameLink`, `frameLinkTrans`, `transitions`, `pptxImport` and `pptxExport`. |
| Change a slide's view (what it shows, when it redraws, what the exports read) | `js/slide-view.js` (`redraw`, `fit`, `fill`, `tidy`, `insert`, `openPicker`); `js/free-frame.js` (`view`, `insertView`, the node view, the `freeFrameViews` plugin); `css/free-frame.css` (the `.pp-ff-view` block); `extensions/diapositives/slide-strip.js` (`pageCopy`, `redrawViews`); `extensions/diapositives/slide-show.js` (`outputHtml` with the anchors `SlideView.shows` asks for); `js/reader-mode.js` (`resolveReaderBody`: `SlideView.fill`); `js/objects-menu.js` (the "Slide view…" row); `js/icons.js` (`slideView`). Tests: `slideView`, `freeFrame`, `frameLayer`, `objectsMenu`, `objectsTableMouse`, `slidesStrip`, `slidesShow`, `frameLink`. |
| Change presentations (the type, the 16:9 page, the "+" menu row, the slide strip) | `js/slides-entry.js` (`SlidesEntry`: the type, `pageFor`, `startNew`, the reason it is greyed out; `ensureFor` and `unavailableReason` for the buttons of the gallery's presentation); `js/templates.js` (`declareExtensionType`; `isOpenableType`: openable when the extension is active; `isKnownType`: never rewritten under another type, never lost); `js/page-layout.js` (`SLIDE_FORMAT`, named "16:9" by `formatLabel`). The slide strip: `extensions/diapositives/slide-strip.js` and `.css`, named by the registry in `js/extensions.js`; it reads the editor's pagination through `HeaderFooterPreview.pageBreaks`, `onPaginated` and `scrollToPage` (`js/header-footer-preview.js`). The page of an empty last slide: `SlidesEntry.showsEveryPage` (the editor, `trailingBlankStart`) and `SlidesEntry.outputHtml` (Reading and exports, from `js/main.js`), which `ReaderMode.trimTrailingBlankBlocks` respects (`js/reader-mode.js`). Linked slides: `js/slide-links.js` (the base) and `extensions/diapositives/slide-links-ui.js` (the "Ask" window, the menu rows). Free objects placed on a slide (the text frame, the shapes, the lines): `js/free-frame.js` (the base) and `js/objects-menu.js` (the top toolbar's "Objects" button). A slide as text of a document: `js/slide-text.js` (the conversion), the "text" mode of the panel in `extensions/diapositives/slide-import.js`, `SlidesEntry.openTextImport` and `pastedInDocument` (the entry and the paste), the `#v2-btn-slide-text` row (`index.html`, `js/main-toolbar.js`). A document page as slides: `extensions/diapositives/page-slides.js` (the conversion and the cut), `SlideText.toSlide` (`js/slide-text.js`), the panel of `extensions/diapositives/slide-import.js` (`importPage`) and the strip's `onPaste` |
| Modify a file in `css/` or `js/` | Bump its `?v=` number in `index.html`, otherwise the browser keeps the old one. |
